# Experiment and design decision log

Read the numerical/measurement [contract](CONTRACT.md) before interpreting these records. Preserve raw evidence, failed attempts, and inconclusive outcomes. An alternative that has not been implemented or measured is not a failed optimization. Historical entries below reconstruct only what the repository supports. Missing prior failure numbers or commit IDs remain unknown.

## E038 — Do the calibrated tensors pass the gate and run at the speed of the plain-rounded ones?

Predeclared 2026-11-11 under the same user request as E037 ("one last attempt"), before E037's full run finished and without knowledge of which variant it selects or what it costs. It closes E036's open item "nothing was timed". It runs whatever E037 selects and saves, pass or fail on E037's quality criteria; the quality figures quoted with E038's timings are E037's for that checkpoint.

Change, one: the VALUES of codes and scales (E037's selected checkpoint, blocks and head) in place of the plain-rounded ones. No kernel, operator, runtime, format, graph or request changes. `scripts/e038/ckpt.py` re-lays-out the checkpoint into E017's artifact format under a new root (symlinks to the old one except `artifacts`), through the unchanged repack with its round-trip assertions, and checks that the FP16 baseline loaded from that root equals the checkpoint's reconstruction bit for bit. Every existing script (E031 `head.py`, E027 `convert.py`/`vbench.py`, E031 `headconv.py`) then runs unchanged with `E017_ROOT` pointing at it; only the head's source of codes is swapped.

One job, one node, contexts 128 and 2048, primary and held-out prompts, two series in reversed order, 3 warmups + 20 requests per cell, 64 tokens, batch 1 — E031's protocol. Eight arms: GQA, GQAH, V, VH on the plain-rounded tensors (E031's arms) and cGQA, cGQAH, cV, cVH on the calibrated ones.

Criteria, fixed now:
1. Gate: E031's `validate` (E021's criteria against an FP64 control on the same weights and the same 4-bit head: finite, tokens, relative L2 ≤ 0.003, graph bitwise equal to eager, repeat bitwise, workspace, state) is accepted in all four cases on the calibrated root. If not, the job stops and no calibrated timing is released. The plain root's gate is rerun in the same job as the control.
2. Equal speed: for each arm and each of the eight cells, the calibrated decode median is within 5% of the plain-rounded median. Reported: the largest absolute difference per arm. "Equal" here means "no difference at the project's 5% threshold", not a proof of equality.
3. Head gain on calibrated tensors: cGQAH at least 5% below cGQA and cVH at least 5% below cV in every cell (E031's criterion).
Reported besides: vLLM's leading-token agreement with our path on the same calibrated weights.

Limits fixed now: the gate is E021's third, post hoc gate (E017 and E019 remain failed) and it shows that the Marlin path computes the calibrated model, not that the calibrated model is good — that is E037's perplexity. No FP16 baseline is timed on the calibrated reconstruction (dense FP16 time does not depend on values; expected, not measured), so "faster than our own FP16" stays E024/E026's statement about plain-rounded weights. Our path remains slower than vLLM. One model, one node, batch 1, 64 tokens, no sanitizer. Marlin, vLLM and its `lm_head` support are upstream. Not a repository default.

## E037 — Which calibrated quantizer in the same 4-bit format costs least, with out-of-domain calibration?

Predeclared 2026-11-11 at the user's request ("i want to give one last one try ... you can use any tool you'd like ... this is one last attempt"; the choice of experiment was left to the agent). This request authorizes the items AGENTS listed after E036 as needing one: other GPTQ settings, out-of-domain calibration data, and keeping a calibrated checkpoint for timing (E038, predeclared separately after this result). Quality only: no runtime, kernel, gate or timing here.

Why this: E036 left three gaps. The blocks fail at +6.77%; calibration was in-domain (WikiText-2 train against WikiText-2 test); the calibrated tensors were not kept, so nothing could be timed. A web search (2026-11-11) found no published WikiText-2 perplexity for a symmetric group-128 4-bit Qwen2.5-1.5B, so nothing here is checked against a reference number. From the llm-compressor documentation (read today): its GPTQ default is "static" activation ordering, described as the best accuracy recovery at no runtime cost, and its guide recommends 512 calibration samples; neither claim comes with a number.

Change, one: how codes and scales are chosen. Runtime format unchanged (symmetric, groups of 128 along K, integer codes in [−7, 7], FP16 scale, reconstruction FP16(q·s)). Variants, written SOURCE/KIND, `scripts/e037/calib.py`, own implementations, no external quantization library:

- SOURCE of the 128 calibration windows of 2048 tokens: W = WikiText-2 train, E036's windows (in-domain); C = the first shard of C4 (`allenai/c4`, `en/c4-train.00000-of-01024.json.gz`, sha256 8ef8d75b…fbc9 fixed before download): the first 128 documents with at least 2048 tokens, their first 2048 tokens (out-of-domain against the WikiText test text).
- KIND G: E036's GPTQ unchanged. A: columns processed in descending Hessian-diagonal order with each scale fixed beforehand for its stored group (llm-compressor's "static"; no permutation is stored). AM: A plus each scale chosen from 21 shrink factors 1.0…0.5 by the |error|^2.4 criterion of the GPTQ reference code (from memory). AMT: AM plus four stages per block (q/k/v, o, gate/up, down) with inputs re-collected after each stage. S: signed-gradient rounding in the style of AutoRound (Cheng et al. 2023, from memory, not re-read): per block, 200 sign-SGD steps, batch 4, step size 1/200 decaying linearly to 0, on one rounding offset per weight in [−0.5, 0.5] and one shrink factor per scale in [0.5, 1], minimizing the squared error of the block's output (inputs from the already quantized blocks) against the original block's output on original inputs; FP16 autocast, straight-through rounding. Deviations from AutoRound as I remember it: batch 4 not 8, codes in [−7, 7], no best-step tracking.
- Run: W/G (reproduction of E036's C/F, expected near 9.188; GPU linear algebra is not bit-reproducible, so reported, not asserted), C/G, C/A, C/AM, C/AMT, C/S. The ladder C/G→A→AM→AMT adds one thing per rung; C/S is a separate method.

Selection, fixed now: every variant is scored with the FP16 head on the WikiText-2 VALIDATION split (pytorch/examples `valid.txt`, non-overlapping 2048-token windows, E032's metric) and on E032's test windows. The C/ variant with the lowest validation perplexity is "the selected checkpoint"; test numbers of the others are reported but select nothing. The selected blocks then get E036's head recipe (kind G) calibrated on their own final hidden states of the C4 windows; blocks and head are saved (`checkpoint.npz`, integer codes and FP16 scales, BeeGFS, hash recorded, not in the repository).

Criteria on the TEST split, E032's labels (negligible ≤1%, modest ≤5%, substantial >5%), for the selected checkpoint only: blocks "pass" if SEL/F is at most 5% above O/F; head "pass" if SEL/GPTQ is at most 5% above SEL/F. Reported besides: whole model against O/F, every variant, W/G against C/G (the in-domain effect). Expectation, stated so it can be wrong: out-of-domain calibration costs something against E036's 6.77%, and it is not likely that the GPTQ ladder alone reaches 5%; S is the variant with a chance.

Checks in the process, also run by the in-job prerequisite (8 calibration windows, 2 windows per split, variants W/G, C/AMT, C/S with 20 steps): plain rounding reproduces E031's head hash; layer replay equals the model forward bit for bit; in a full run O/F and in-process plain rounding of the 196 projections reproduce E032's O and R to 1e-5; reloading the selected checkpoint from its saved codes and scales reproduces its sweep score exactly; codes within [−7, 7], finite. Not a stop condition, recorded per layer: GPTQ calibration output error relative to plain rounding (with fixed scales it is not guaranteed below 1); for S the loss at start and end and the count of non-finite gradient elements (zeroed).

Limits fixed now: one model, one metric, one test corpus, one run, one seed for S; validation and test are both WikiText-2, so selection is in-domain for the test even though calibration is not; the head recipe is not searched; nothing is timed or gated (E038).

### E037 result — best out-of-domain blocks +5.62% (fail, down from E036's in-domain +6.77%); head +1.57% (pass) (2026-11-11, c4)

Job 335301 on c4, third attempt, exit 0, 11:23–13:28 EST. `results/e037_20261111/calib-c4-335301/` (`quality.json` with every arm and per-layer records, `arms.json`, logs, the in-job prerequisite, `all.sha256`). The prerequisite passed and the full run followed: 128 calibration windows per source (C4: the first 128 documents with at least 2048 tokens among the first 4,179 of the shard), validation 132 windows / 270,204 scored tokens, test 150 windows / 307,050. Controls: O 8.605813 and in-process plain rounding 9.734987 reproduce E032 to 1e-5; W/G gives 9.1882, E036's 9.188. Every GPTQ arm equals attempt 2's log line to all four printed decimals. The selected checkpoint reloaded from its saved codes and scales scores bit-identically.

| Variant (blocks; FP16 head) | Validation ppl | Validation rise | Test ppl | Test rise vs original | Test top-1 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| O original | 8.9684 | — | 8.6058 | — | 53.86% |
| plain rounding (E017's) | 10.2168 | +13.92% | 9.7350 | +13.12% | 52.06% |
| W/G in-domain, E036's quantizer | 9.6088 | +7.14% | 9.1882 | +6.77% | 53.00% |
| C/G C4, E036's quantizer | 9.7689 | +8.93% | 9.3425 | +8.56% | 52.68% |
| C/A | 9.5394 | +6.37% | 9.1288 | +6.08% | 52.90% |
| C/M | 9.6457 | +7.55% | 9.2305 | +7.26% | 52.94% |
| C/T | 9.7036 | +8.20% | 9.2747 | +7.77% | 52.75% |
| C/+ = AMT | 9.7247 | +8.43% | 9.3411 | +8.54% | 52.77% |
| C/AM | 9.7305 | +8.50% | 9.3179 | +8.27% | 52.83% |
| **C/AT (selected: lowest validation)** | **9.4933** | **+5.85%** | **9.0894** | **+5.62%** | 53.04% |
| C/MT | 9.6238 | +7.31% | 9.2200 | +7.14% | 52.93% |
| C/S sign-SGD rounding | 9.7412 | +8.62% | 9.3158 | +8.25% | 52.88% |
| **C/AT blocks + calibrated head** | 9.6511 | head +1.66% | 9.2319 | **head +1.57%**; whole model +7.28% | 52.70% |

- Block criterion (selected C/AT at most 5% above O/F on test): **fail**, +5.62%, "substantial". Say +5.62% or "5.6%", never "about 5%".
- Head criterion (calibrated head at most 5% above the FP16 head on the selected blocks): **pass**, +1.57%, "modest". With in-domain calibration E036 measured +0.89–1.03%; say "about 1.6%" for this checkpoint.
- Whole model, selected blocks and head: +7.28% over the original, against +22.84% for plain rounding and +7.72% for E036's in-domain checkpoint, in the identical runtime format.
- Calibration source, same quantizer: C4 costs 1.79 points more than in-domain (8.56% against 6.77%). The selected out-of-domain variant is nevertheless 1.15 points better than E036's in-domain result.
- A, M and T each help alone; A with T is the best pair; every combination containing both A and M is no better than C/G, and only those two variants have calibration output error above plain rounding's anywhere (`down_proj` of blocks 2 and 26, ratio up to 1.78). Observation; the cause (scales fixed in advance and also shrunk, so compensated weights clip) is a hypothesis, not tested.
- Validation and test rank the eleven calibrated variants in the same order except for neighbours within 0.3 points; the variant selected on validation is also the best on test.
- S (signed-gradient rounding, own implementation): the per-block loss falls (end/start 0.32–0.84, median 0.63, in blocks 1–27; 1.18 in block 0; zero non-finite gradients) but the model is no better than C/G. This does NOT measure AutoRound: it is my implementation from memory with a different loss (per-token relative), batch 4, one seed, no best-step tracking, no tuning. Recorded as "this implementation did not help", not as a result about the published method.

What this does not show: one model, one metric, one test corpus, one run; validation and test are both WikiText-2, so the selection is in-domain for the test even though calibration is not; eleven variants were scored on test and three of them were added after a table with test numbers was seen (attempt 2), with the selection rule unchanged; the head recipe was not searched; NOTHING IS TIMED OR GATED here (E038 does that for this checkpoint). GPTQ and activation ordering are upstream methods (Frantar et al.; llm-compressor's "static" ordering); ours is the implementation in the project format, the comparison and the measurement. The checkpoint (`checkpoint.npz`, sha256 bb161620…ba93b, 1.57 GB) stays on BeeGFS. Not a repository default. Untested, not authorized: more calibration windows, mixed calibration sources, group sizes other than 128, the upstream AutoRound or llm-compressor libraries, other damping.

### E037 attempt 2 — full run crashed at the last variant, out of GPU memory; no result file (2026-11-11, c4, job 335226)

Exit 1 after 1 h 52 min. `results/e037_20261111/attempt-c4-335226-oom/` (logs, the prerequisite's `quality.json`). The prerequisite passed (all seven variants; S with the amended loss fell in every block but block 0, zero non-finite gradients). The full run scored six variants, finished all 28 blocks of C/S, and died allocating the 1.2 GB logits tensor while scoring C/S (7.9 GiB allocated, 6.3 GiB reserved but unusable). `quality.json` and the checkpoint are written at the end, so neither exists; the numbers below are the four-decimal `ARM` lines of `calib.log`, and C/S has no score.

| Variant (128 windows; FP16 head) | Validation ppl | Test ppl | Test rise vs original |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| O original | 8.9684 | 8.6058 | — |
| plain rounding | 10.2168 | 9.7350 | +13.12% |
| W/G (in-domain, E036's quantizer) | 9.6088 | 9.1882 | +6.77% |
| C/G (C4, E036's quantizer) | 9.7689 | 9.3425 | +8.56% |
| C/A | 9.5394 | 9.1288 | +6.08% |
| C/M | 9.6457 | 9.2305 | +7.26% |
| C/T | 9.7036 | 9.2747 | +7.77% |
| C/+ = AMT | 9.7247 | 9.3411 | +8.54% |

Observed: the controls reproduce E032 (8.6058, 9.7350) and W/G reproduces E036 (9.188). Out-of-domain calibration costs 1.8 points with the unchanged quantizer (8.56% against 6.77%). Each of A, M, T alone lowers both validation and test perplexity, but their combination is no better than none of them: the factors are not additive. Not explained; one candidate, untested, is that fixed scales that have also been shrunk clip the compensated weights. S: per-block loss at the end over the start 0.31–0.84 in blocks 1–27, 1.18 in block 0.

Amendment before attempt 3 (commit follows): memory only — the head's FP32 copy leaves the GPU until needed, each variant's weights are reloaded from their saved codes after a cache release before scoring, `PYTORCH_CUDA_ALLOC_CONF=expandable_segments:True`; the table is written after every variant. Added variants, chosen AFTER seeing the table above including its test column (disclosed; the selection rule still reads only the validation column): the three pairs C/AM, C/AT, C/MT, because the combination rule produced only the triple and the triple failed. Run order: W/G, C/G, C/A, C/M, C/T, C/+, C/AM, C/AT, C/MT, C/S. The prerequisite runs C/G, C/AM, C/S (10 steps); it cannot reproduce the memory failure, which needs full-size calibration.

### E037 attempt 1 — stopped after the in-job prerequisite; S diverged (2026-11-11, c7, job 335222)

The prerequisite (8 calibration windows, 2 windows per split, 20 sign-SGD steps) ran W/G, C/AMT and six blocks of C/S; I cancelled the job by hand before the full run. `scancel` ended the job without its retrieval wait, so the run directory (`calib-c7-335222` on BeeGFS) was retrieved during the next job; the numbers below were read from `smoke.log` while the job ran. No full-run number exists from this attempt.

- Observed, C/S: the block loss ROSE in four of six blocks (block 1: 5.4e5 at the start, 5.1e6 at the end; block 3: 1.9e6 to 3.3e6) and 1.1e8 gradient elements were non-finite in block 1. The plain squared error is of order 1e6 from block 1 on, where it was 2e4 in block 0. Interpretation, not separately measured: a few token positions carry activations far larger than the rest in this model family from the second block on, they own a plain squared error, and their gradients overflow FP16.
- Observed, 2 windows per split, not comparable with full runs: O 8.009, plain rounding 9.077, W/G 8.547 (+6.7%), C/AMT 9.040 (+12.9%), i.e. C/AMT barely better than plain rounding, with calibration output error ABOVE plain rounding's in some `down_proj` (ratio 1.44, 1.62). With 8 windows this cannot separate the calibration source from the A/M/T settings.

Amendment before any full run (commit follows this entry):
- S now runs in FP32 (the block is cast to FP32 for the steps and back, exact for FP16 values) and minimizes the PER-TOKEN RELATIVE error ‖y−t‖²/‖t‖² averaged over tokens, so no position owns the loss. This departs from AutoRound's plain squared error; the final hidden state passes a per-token normalization before the head, which is the argument for a per-token relative measure.
- The ladder is replaced by single factors, because a bad first rung would hide the later ones: C/G, C/A, C/M (shrunken scales, E036's order), C/T (staged, E036's order and scales), then C/+ = the combination of those of A, M, T that alone lower VALIDATION perplexity below C/G (not run if fewer than two do), then C/S. W/G is kept. Selection rule, criteria and limits are unchanged. The prerequisite now runs every variant (10 sign-SGD steps).

## E036 — Does a calibrated quantizer in the same 4-bit format remove the quality cost?

Predeclared 2026-11-11 at the user's request ("i guess one last test should be fine", answering the proposal of exactly this experiment) after E032/E034: plain rounding costs +13.12% perplexity in the blocks and +8.60% in the head, and no uncalibrated 4-bit head reached 5%. Quality only: no runtime, kernel, gate or timing. This is the calibrated quantizer that AGENTS listed as needing a user request.

Change, one: how codes and scales are chosen. The runtime format is unchanged (symmetric, groups of 128 along K, integer codes in [−7, 7], FP16 scale, reconstruction FP16(q·s)), so the tensors remain loadable by the Marlin operator and vLLM paths timed in E020–E035. The quantizer is GPTQ (Frantar et al. 2022, cited from memory, not re-read): columns are rounded one at a time and the remaining columns are compensated through the inverse Hessian H = Σxᵀx of the layer's inputs, damping 1% of the mean diagonal; each group's scale is max|w|/7 of the already compensated weights. Own implementation, `scripts/e036/gptq.py`; no external GPTQ library. Blocks are calibrated in order, each on the outputs of the already quantized blocks before it, all seven projections of a block from one pass. The head is calibrated on the final hidden states of the blocks it is paired with. Calibration: 128 evenly spaced 2048-token windows of the WikiText-2 TRAIN split (pytorch/examples raw file).

Measurement: E032's test text, windows and metric, one process, PyTorch FP16 on reconstructed weights. Arms (blocks/head): O/F, R/F, R/G128 (controls: must reproduce E032's O, R, RH to 1e-5 relative); R/GPTQ (plain-rounded blocks, calibrated head); C/F (calibrated blocks, FP16 head); C/GPTQ (both calibrated).

Checks inside the process, also run by the in-job prerequisite (8 calibration windows, 2 test windows) before the full run: the script's plain-rounding function reproduces E031's head bit for bit (hash); the layer-by-layer replay used for calibration equals the model's own forward bit for bit; for each of the 196 projections and both heads the calibration output error tr(ΔW·H·ΔWᵀ) of GPTQ is not above plain rounding's (a failure stops the job; it is not a guaranteed property of the algorithm, so a stop here would be a finding, not a bug by assumption).

Criteria, fixed now, with E032's labels (negligible ≤1%, modest ≤5%, substantial >5%): blocks "pass" if C/F is at most 5% above O/F; head "pass" if the calibrated head is at most 5% above the FP16 head on the same blocks, required for both R/GPTQ against R/F and C/GPTQ against C/F. Reported besides: C/GPTQ against O/F, top-1 accuracy, per-layer errors.

Limits fixed now: calibration and test are the two splits of ONE corpus, so calibration is in-domain and the result may flatter the quantizer on other text; out-of-domain calibration is untested. One model, one metric, one seedless deterministic sample choice, one run (GPU linear algebra is not guaranteed bit-reproducible; hashes of the reconstructed weights identify this run only, and the weights themselves are not kept). No timing: speed is expected, not measured, to equal the plain-rounded tensors' because format and shapes are identical. No token-level or logit gate against anything; nothing here changes E017/E019/E021. Not a repository default.

### E036 result — head passes (+0.9–1.0%), blocks fail (+6.77%, down from +13.12%) (2026-11-11, c10)

Job 334671 on c10, exit 0 on the first attempt, no failed attempt, 00:13–00:31 EST. `results/e036_20261111/gptq-c10-334671/` (`quality.json` with per-layer records, `top1.npz`, logs, the in-job smoke, `all.sha256`). The in-job prerequisite (8 calibration windows, 2 test windows) passed every in-process check and the full run followed: 128 calibration windows (262,144 tokens of the train split, stride 19,915 tokens), 150 test windows, 307,050 scored tokens. The three controls reproduce E032 to every printed digit (O 8.605813, R 9.734987, RH 10.571791).

| Arm (blocks / head) | Perplexity | Top-1 accuracy | Compared with | Rise | Label |
|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---|
| O/F: original | 8.606 | 53.86% | — | — | — |
| R/F: plain-rounded blocks, FP16 head (control) | 9.735 | 52.06% | O/F | +13.12% | substantial |
| **C/F: calibrated blocks, FP16 head** | 9.188 | 53.00% | O/F | **+6.77%** | substantial |
| R/G128: plain-rounded blocks and head (control) | 10.572 | 50.87% | R/F | +8.60% | substantial |
| **R/GPTQ: plain-rounded blocks, calibrated head** | 9.835 | 51.85% | R/F | **+1.03%** | modest |
| **C/GPTQ: calibrated blocks and head** | 9.270 | 52.87% | C/F | **+0.89%** | negligible |
| C/GPTQ, whole model | 9.270 | 52.87% | O/F | +7.72% | substantial |
| R/G128, whole model (control) | 10.572 | 50.87% | O/F | +22.84% | substantial |

- Head criterion (at most 5% above the FP16 head, on both block sets): **pass**, +1.03% and +0.89% against +8.60% for plain rounding. One arm is 0.03 points above the 1% "negligible" line, so the head's label is "modest"; say "about 1%", not "negligible".
- Block criterion (C/F at most 5% above O/F): **fail**, +6.77%. Calibration removes about half of the block cost (13.12% to 6.77%) but the result is still "substantial".
- Whole model with both calibrated: +7.72% over the original, against +22.84% with plain rounding, in the identical runtime format.
- Mechanism check, observed: GPTQ's calibration output error is below plain rounding's in all 196 projections (ratio 0.027–0.607, median 0.394) and both heads (0.113–0.118), while the head's WEIGHT error rises (0.1218 to 0.1403–0.1406). The quantizer trades weight fidelity for output fidelity on the calibration inputs, which is why E034's weight-error-minimizing search could not find this.

What this does not show: calibration and test are two splits of one corpus (in-domain), so these numbers may flatter the quantizer on other text; one model, one metric, one run; the calibrated tensors were not kept (hashes in `quality.json` identify this run only) and NOTHING WAS TIMED: that Marlin or vLLM decode these tensors at the speeds of E020–E035 is expected from the identical format and shapes, not measured, and no token or logit gate was applied to them. Untested, not authorized: more calibration data, out-of-domain calibration, block-sequential ordering inside a block, other damping, timing the calibrated checkpoint. GPTQ is upstream work (Frantar et al.); ours is this implementation in the project format and the measurement. Not a repository default.

## E035 — How much of the head speedup survives with an 8-bit head (vLLM)?

Predeclared 2026-11-10 at the user's request ("go ahead") after E034: no uncalibrated 4-bit head reached 5% perplexity cost, while the 8-bit group-128 head (B8) cost +0.04%. This times that head. vLLM only: our operator executes 4-bit codes only, so our own path has no 8-bit arm.

Change: one checkpoint, the E027 AutoGPTQ checkpoint (196 block projections, project G128 codes, unchanged) plus an 8-bit head: symmetric, group 128, scale FP16(max/127), codes q+128 packed four per word, selected for `lm_head` alone through the GPTQ config's `dynamic` per-module override (`scripts/e035/head8conv.py`). Source reading of the pinned v0.33.0 wheel says the loader maps (8 bits, symmetric) to `uint8b128` and honours `dynamic`; that it does so for the head is verified by the smoke, not assumed. The converter asserts the head's weight error equals E034's B8 (0.0067225 ± 1e-5).

Measurement: stock vLLM (V), vLLM with the 4-bit head (VH, E031's checkpoint) and with the 8-bit head (VH8), interleaved in one job on one node; contexts 128 and 2048, two passages, two reversed series, 3 warmups + 20 requests, decode = 64-token minus 1-token wall time, as in E031/E033 (`scripts/e027/submit.sh e035`, `scripts/e035/analyze.py`). References from `head.py validate` rerun in the job. In-job prerequisite before any timing: one VH8 cell must load, reproduce the first reference token and agree with the FP16-head reference on at least 32 leading tokens.

Criterion, fixed now: "pass" if VH8's decode median is at least 5% below V's in all eight cells. Reported besides: VH against V, the share of VH's gain that VH8 retains, and VH8's leading-token agreement with the FP16-head tokens of our GQA path. Expected, not measured: VH8 lands between V and VH, because the head reads twice the weight bytes of the 4-bit one.

Limits fixed now: the 8-bit head's correctness evidence is token agreement plus E034's perplexity of the same reconstruction in PyTorch FP16; there is no logit-level check of vLLM's 8-bit kernel against that reconstruction and no E021-style gate (no project arm exists). One node, batch 1, 64 tokens, one model. Quality: +0.04% perplexity over the FP16 head on WikiText-2, on blocks that are themselves 13.1% above the FP16 original (E032). Marlin, vLLM and its 8-bit and per-module support are upstream; ours is the head quantization, conversion and measurement.

### E035 result — pass: the 8-bit head keeps about 70% of the head speedup, 15.6–16.5% below stock vLLM, with all 64 tokens unchanged (2026-11-10, c51)

Job 334367 on c51, exit 0 on the first attempt, no failed attempt. `results/e035_20261110/e035-c51-334367/` (`analysis.json`, 24 cell records plus the smoke cell, logs, `all.sha256` covering the files left on BeeGFS). Conversion: head weight error 0.0067225, equal to E034's B8. In-job prerequisite passed: vLLM loaded the 8-bit head through the `dynamic` override (the quarter-size-per-word tensors would not load under the 4-bit setting) and the smoke cell reproduced 64 of 64 FP16-head reference tokens. All four `head.py validate` cases accepted. 3 warmups + 20 requests per cell.

| Context | V: FP16 head, ms | VH: 4-bit head, ms | VH8: 8-bit head, ms | VH below V | VH8 below V | Share of VH's gain kept |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 128 | 233.6–233.7 | 178.5–178.6 | 195.2 | 23.6% | 16.4–16.5% | 69.7–69.8% |
| 2048 | 244.1–244.5 | 189.1–189.3 | 205.9–206.1 | 22.5–22.6% | 15.6–15.7% | 69.3–69.8% |

- Criterion met in all eight cells (minimum 15.62%): **pass**. VH8 sits between V and VH as expected.
- VH8 reproduced all 64 tokens of the FP16-head reference (our GQA path) in every cell and every request, where the 4-bit head changed tokens in one of four E031 cases.
- Combined with E034: in vLLM an 8-bit group-128 head decodes 15.6–16.5% faster than the FP16 head at a measured perplexity cost of +0.04% over that head, against 22.5–23.6% faster at +8.6% for the 4-bit head. This is the first head result whose quality cost is "negligible" under E032's labels.

What this does not show: no project arm (our operator is 4-bit only, so our own path gains nothing here); no logit-level check of vLLM's 8-bit kernel against the reconstruction and no E021-style gate, only token agreement plus E034's perplexity of the same weights in PyTorch FP16; no same-node comparison with ONNX Runtime or llama.cpp (c51's V is 233.6 ms against c10's 238.1 ms, so cross-node comparison with E033's ORT numbers is not made); batch 1, 64 tokens, one model, one node, one corpus for quality. The blocks remain 13.1% above the FP16 original in perplexity (E032). Marlin, vLLM, its 8-bit kernel and per-module override are upstream; ours is the head quantization, conversion and measurement. Not a repository default.

## E034 — Can a better head quantizer keep the 4-bit head's speed at a smaller quality cost?

Predeclared 2026-11-10 at the user's request ("run those multiple tests concurrently if you can") after E032 labelled the E031 head "substantial" (+8.60% perplexity). Quality only: no runtime, kernel, gate or timing. The blocks stay the project G128 reconstruction (R) in every variant; only the output head changes.

Method: E032's corpus, tokenization, 2048-token windows and metric, unchanged. One process (`scripts/e034/heads.py`): the 4-bit blocks run once per window and each head variant is applied to the same final hidden states in PyTorch FP16, which is how the "concurrent" request is met on one GPU. Variants, all symmetric, groups along K, reconstruction FP16(q·s) with FP16 scales:

| Variant | Head | Runtime format |
|---|---|---|
| F | FP16 (control) | — |
| G128 | E031's head: group 128, scale max/7 (control) | as timed in E031 |
| G128M | group 128, scale c·max/7, c ∈ {1.00, 0.95, …, 0.50} chosen per group and column to minimize squared weight error | identical to G128 |
| G32 | group 32, scale max/7 | four times the scales |
| G32M | group 32 with the same search | as G32 |
| B8 | group 128, 8-bit, scale max/127 | none of ours; upper bound only |

Controls, fixed now: the in-process G128 reconstruction must equal `scripts/e017/quantize.py` bit for bit and hash-equal E031's head (asserted before any scoring); F and G128 must reproduce E032's R and RH perplexities (9.735, 10.572) within 0.01%, otherwise the run is invalid. A two-window smoke of the same script precedes the full run in the job.

Reported per variant: relative weight error, perplexity, top-1 accuracy, perplexity rise against F from the same run, top-1 agreement with F. Labels are E032's, unchanged: "negligible" ≤ 1%, "modest" ≤ 5%, "substantial" above. Hypothesis: at least one 4-bit variant is "modest" or better. If so, the one with the least format change (order G128M, G32, G32M) is the candidate for a separate, user-requested gate-and-timing experiment; nothing here makes E031's claim unconditional, because E031 timed the G128 head only. If none is, the result is that these uncalibrated fixes are insufficient; a calibrated quantizer stays untested.

Limits fixed now: one model, one corpus, one metric; the search minimizes weight error, not output error, and uses no calibration data; choosing among variants on the test corpus is a selection on that corpus (five variants, disclosed); B8 has no runtime in our operator and no timing anywhere; whether the pinned Marlin build accepts a group-32 head, and what it costs in time, is unverified.

### E034 result — no uncalibrated 4-bit head reaches 5%; the best is 5.8%; an 8-bit head costs 0.04% (2026-11-10, c51)

Attempts: job 334362 (c51) stopped in its two-window smoke at the bit-for-bit control ("G128 differs from the project quantizer"), before any scoring; logs in `results/e034_20261110/attempt-334362-negative-zero/`. Change: `+ 0.0` after rounding, so a weight that rounds to −0 reconstructs as +0 as in `scripts/e017/quantize.py`. That this was the cause is inferred from the control passing afterwards; the differing elements were not dumped. Job 334363 (c51), exit 0: smoke and full run. Evidence `results/e034_20261110/` (`quality.json`, `top1.npz`).

Controls pass: G128 is bit-identical to the project quantizer and hash-equal to E031's head; F and G128 reproduce E032's R and RH perplexities to every printed digit (9.734987, 10.571791). 307,050 scored positions, 150 windows.

| Head (blocks 4-bit G128 in all) | Weight error | Perplexity | Rise vs FP16 head | Label | Top-1 same as FP16 head |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|
| F: FP16 | 0 | 9.735 | — | — | 100% |
| G128: E031's head | 0.1218 | 10.572 | +8.60% | substantial | 84.09% |
| G128M: group 128, error-minimizing scales | 0.1102 | 10.504 | +7.90% | substantial | 83.69% |
| G32: group 32 | 0.0987 | 10.509 | +7.95% | substantial | 83.42% |
| G32M: group 32, error-minimizing scales | 0.0935 | 10.301 | +5.82% | substantial | 84.97% |
| B8: 8-bit, group 128 | 0.0067 | 9.738 | +0.04% | negligible | 98.83% |

- The hypothesis is rejected: no 4-bit variant is "modest". G32M comes closest at +5.82%, above the 5% line; it is not rounded down. No candidate goes forward to a gate-and-timing experiment, and E031's statement keeps its 8.6% cost.
- Weight error and perplexity do not move together: G128M and G32 cut the weight error by 10% and 19% but the perplexity rise only from 8.60% to about 7.9%, and both agree with the FP16 head's top-1 slightly less often than G128 does. Lower squared weight error is a weak proxy for output quality here (observation; not explained).
- The 8-bit head is indistinguishable from FP16 on this metric (+0.04%). It shows the loss is a matter of bits in the head, not of quantizing it at all. No runtime of ours executes an 8-bit head and no timing of one exists, so this is a quality bound, not a speed result.

Limits as predeclared: one corpus, one metric; weight-error search without calibration data; five variants compared on the test corpus. Untested, not authorized: a calibrated head quantizer (GPTQ/AWQ-style, output-error-aware), an 8-bit head timed in vLLM (its Marlin build's 8-bit support for the head is unverified), mixed precision.

## E033 — vLLM with the 4-bit head: same-job comparison with ONNX Runtime, and batch sizes 1–8

Predeclared 2026-11-10 after the user invited concurrent runs while E032 is in progress. Two open points from E031, no change to any path:

1. E031 compared vLLM+head with ONNX Runtime INT4 across separate jobs. Here stock vLLM (V), vLLM with the project 4-bit head (VH) and ORT INT4 (OQ) are interleaved in one job on one node, contexts 128 and 2048, two passages, two reversed series, 3 warmups + 20 requests. Outcome per context, fixed now, VH against OQ: "faster" if VH is ≥5% below OQ in all four cells, "slower" if OQ is ≥5% below VH in all four, "tie" if all are within ±5%, otherwise "mixed". Different quantizers; stack against stack.
2. Every full-model measurement so far is batch 1. V and VH are also run with 2, 4 and 8 identical concurrent requests (primary passage, both contexts, both series). Hypothesis: VH's decode median is ≥5% below V's at every batch size. Expected, not measured: the percentage shrinks as the batch grows, because the FP16 head becomes a multi-row GEMM. Reported: decode time for the 63 steps of the whole batch, tokens per second (63 × batch / decode time), the gain, and the smallest number of leading tokens any sequence of a batch shares with the batch-1 reference (batched kernels take different tile paths, so later tokens may differ; only the first token is required to match).

References and correctness come from `head.py validate` rerun in the job (E031's gate). Limits fixed now: identical prompts in a batch (no mix of lengths), vLLM only for the batch sweep (our path is batch-1 only; ORT's batch API is not exercised), decode measured as the 64-token minus 1-token wall-time difference, one node (the one that already holds both node-local environments), E031's output-change caveat and E032's pending quality result apply unchanged. `scripts/e027/submit.sh e033`, `scripts/e033/analyze.py`.

### E033 result — vLLM with the 4-bit head ties ONNX Runtime INT4 in the same job; the head gain holds to batch 8 (2026-11-10, c10)

Job 334360 on c10, exit 0 on the first attempt, 19:43–20:17 EST. `results/e033_20261110/e033-c10-334360/` (`analysis.json`, 48 cell records, logs, `all.sha256` of every file including the logit archives and compile cache left on BeeGFS). All four `head.py validate` cases accepted under E031's gate. Every cell has 3 warmups + 20 requests.

1. vLLM+head against ORT INT4, interleaved. Decode medians for 63 tokens: context 128, VH 180.7–180.9 ms, OQ 185.0–185.3 ms, VH lower by 2.30–2.39%; context 2048, VH 190.9–191.4 ms, OQ 191.7–191.8 ms, VH lower by 0.20–0.41%. All eight cells are within ±5%: outcome **"tie"** at both contexts. This confirms E031's cross-job reading with a same-job measurement; it is not a win. Different quantizers, stack against stack; the VH model carries E032's +8.6% perplexity cost and ORT's INT4 model has no quality measurement here.
2. Head gain in vLLM by batch size (primary passage, identical prompts in a batch, two series):

| Context | Batch | V decode ms | VH decode ms | V tokens/s | VH tokens/s | VH below V (s1 / s2) |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 128 | 1 | 238.1–238.3 | 180.7–180.7 | 264.6–264.4 | 348.6–348.6 | 24.1% / 24.1% |
| 128 | 2 | 239.0–239.0 | 185.0–185.0 | 527.1–527.2 | 681.1–681.2 | 22.6% / 22.6% |
| 128 | 4 | 239.7–240.7 | 186.7–186.7 | 1051.5–1047.0 | 1349.6–1349.4 | 22.1% / 22.4% |
| 128 | 8 | 243.0–242.6 | 188.8–189.0 | 2074.4–2077.2 | 2669.2–2666.4 | 22.3% / 22.1% |
| 2048 | 1 | 248.8–249.0 | 190.9–191.2 | 253.2–253.0 | 330.0–329.5 | 23.3% / 23.2% |
| 2048 | 2 | 262.4–262.5 | 208.2–209.1 | 480.2–479.9 | 605.2–602.5 | 20.7% / 20.3% |
| 2048 | 4 | 280.2–281.3 | 226.0–225.5 | 899.2–895.8 | 1114.9–1117.5 | 19.3% / 19.8% |
| 2048 | 8 | 336.9–336.2 | 281.6–282.0 | 1496.0–1499.0 | 1789.9–1787.4 | 16.4% / 16.1% |

The hypothesis holds: VH is at least 5% below V at every batch size and context (16.1–24.1%). As expected, the percentage shrinks with batch, mildly at context 128 (24.1% to 22.1%) and more at 2048 (23.3% to 16.1%). Every sequence of every batch reproduced all 64 reference tokens. Observation, not explained: at context 128 the whole batch decodes in nearly the batch-1 time (throughput 7.8 times higher at batch 8), while at 2048 batch 8 takes 35% longer than batch 1.

Limits as predeclared: identical prompts per batch, vLLM only (our path is batch-1 only), one node, 64 tokens. The batch code path of `vbench.py` ran here for the first time; its only checks are the token agreement and the batch-1 cells reproducing E031's medians (V 237.4–238.6 then, 238.1–238.7 now; VH 180.7 both times). The 4-bit head's speedup still CHANGES MODEL OUTPUTS and costs +8.6% perplexity (E032); E034 found no uncalibrated 4-bit head under 5%.

## E032 — Output quality of the quantized model, with and without the 4-bit head

Predeclared 2026-11-10 at the user's request ("go ahead") after E031, whose speedup is conditional on this. It is also the first quality measurement of the block quantization used since E017. No runtime, kernel or timing is involved.

Method: teacher-forced perplexity on the WikiText-2 test split (the plain-text file distributed with the PyTorch examples, hash recorded at first download), tokenized once without special tokens, non-overlapping 2048-token windows over the whole text, next-token NLL summed in FP64 over every scored position, perplexity = exp(mean). Four models, one process each, PyTorch FP16 arithmetic on reconstructed weights (`scripts/e032/quality.py`): O original FP16; R the 196 block projections replaced by their project-G128 reconstruction; OH and RH the same with the output head replaced by its G128 reconstruction, which must hash-equal the head timed in E031. Marlin and vLLM are not run: E021/E031 already bound their deviation from these weights' exact computation to relative L2 ≤ 0.003, which is taken as negligible for perplexity (an assumption, stated).

Reported: perplexity and top-1 next-token accuracy of all four, the relative perplexity rise from the head (RH versus R, and OH versus O), from the blocks (R versus O) and from both, and the share of positions where the top-1 prediction is unchanged. Labels fixed now for the head's effect, RH versus R: "negligible" if perplexity rises ≤ 1%, "modest" if ≤ 5%, "substantial" above that. The same labels are applied to R versus O for context. E031's conditional claim becomes unconditional only on "negligible" or "modest", and then only with the measured rise quoted.

Limits fixed now: one model, one corpus, one metric; perplexity on a tokenized-style Wikipedia text is not a task evaluation; the quantizer is plain per-group max-scaling round-to-nearest with no calibration, so a poor result would describe this quantizer, not 4-bit head quantization in general.

### E032 result — the 4-bit head raises perplexity 8.6%: "substantial"; the block quantization alone costs 13.1% (2026-11-10, c10)

Job 334359 on c10, exit 0 on the first attempt, no failed attempt. `results/e032_20261110/` (`analysis.json`, per-variant records and top-1 arrays). WikiText-2 test file 1,256,449 bytes, hash recorded; 307,050 scored positions in 150 windows; the head reconstruction hash equals E031's.

| Model | Perplexity | Top-1 next-token accuracy |
|---|---:|---:|
| O: original FP16 | 8.606 | 53.86% |
| OH: original blocks, 4-bit head | 9.297 | 52.72% |
| R: 4-bit blocks, FP16 head (the model of E017–E030) | 9.735 | 52.06% |
| RH: 4-bit blocks and 4-bit head (the model of E031) | 10.572 | 50.87% |

- Head effect, the predeclared comparison RH versus R: perplexity +8.60% (and +8.03% on the original blocks). Above the 5% line: **"substantial"**. The top-1 prediction changes at 15.9% of positions.
- Block quantization, R versus O: +13.12%, also "substantial"; top-1 changes at 17.8% of positions. Both together: +22.85%, top-1 changes at 23.8% of positions.

Consequence, as fixed beforehand: E031's speedup does NOT become unconditional. With this quantizer, the 23–24% decode gain in vLLM (15–17% in our path) is bought with a measured 8.6% perplexity increase on top of a model that is already 13.1% worse than FP16. Every statement of E031's gain must now quote that cost. The same applies in retrospect to every full-model Marlin speedup since E020: they compare runtimes on a 4-bit model whose perplexity is 13.1% above the FP16 original's; the FP16 baselines R/GR/GRA ran those same reconstructed weights, so the speed comparisons remain like for like, but "4-bit versus the original model" carries that quality cost.

Limits: one corpus, one metric, one model; plain per-group max-scaling round-to-nearest at group 128 with no calibration or clipping search, which is the weakest common 4-bit quantizer, so this bounds this quantizer and not 4-bit quantization of these matrices in general (llama.cpp and ONNX Runtime use other schemes whose quality was not measured here either). Untested ideas, not authorized: smaller groups or error-minimizing scales for the head, a calibrated quantizer (GPTQ/AWQ), an 8-bit head.

## E031 — 4-bit output head through Marlin, in our path and in vLLM

Predeclared 2026-11-10 from the E030 result. The user agreed beforehand to the experiment the profile would select and asked that follow-ups be queued on the kept nodes; E030 selects this one because the FP16 output head (1169 µs per step) is the largest single kernel in both stacks and the one cost vLLM has not removed. One main change: the output projection (the tied 151,936 × 1536 embedding matrix, cast to FP16 as both runtimes load it) is quantized with the unchanged project G128 quantizer and executed by Marlin. The FP16 embedding lookup, the 196 block projections and everything else are unchanged. No kernel change.

**This changes the model's outputs**, unlike every earlier full-model experiment, because the head was FP16 in the reference model. Two questions are therefore kept separate:
1. Does the runtime compute the head-quantized model correctly? Gate, fixed now: E021's rule unchanged, applied to graph-Marlin with direct attention and Marlin head (GQAH) against an FP64 control that uses the same reconstructed head weights (and FP64 block projections), per case at 64 tokens; plus bitwise equality with eager, bitwise repeat, workspace guards, state. Third-gate disclosure applies as before.
2. How much does quantizing the head change what the model produces? Reported, not gated, and NOT a quality evaluation: number of leading tokens (of 64) the head-quantized control shares with the FP16-head control, logit relative L2 between the two controls at the first step and over the shared steps, and the head's weight quantization error. No perplexity or task metric is measured; no claim that the change is acceptable follows from this experiment.

Arms and hypotheses, each with the usual criterion (≥5% lower decode median in every passage × series cell of a context):
- Ours: GQAH versus GQA (`scripts/e031/head.py`).
- vLLM: VH versus V, where VH is vLLM v0.33.0 loading the E027 checkpoint plus the same head codes/scales marked `lm_head: true` (`scripts/e031/headconv.py`); whether vLLM accepts a quantized head for this model is unknown and a refusal is recorded as a failed arm. VH's first token must equal GQAH's in every request; leading-token agreement is reported.
Expected size (calculation from E030 and the E025 Marlin cost per weight, not a result): roughly 0.6–0.8 ms per step saved, about 12–15% of our step and 16–21% of vLLM's.

Contexts 128 and 2048 × primary/held-out × two reversed series, 3 warmups + 20 requests, one process per cell, all four variants on one node. Also reported: GQAH versus V and VH. Smoke (context128 validation, one cell of GQAH, V, VH) inspected before the dependent full job. Limits fixed now: one model, batch 1, 64 tokens, two passages; the Marlin operator and its workspace have never been validated at 151,936 output columns, so the gate and workspace guards at that width are part of what the smoke tests; prefill also runs the head over every prompt position in our path.

Execution notes: smoke `head-smoke-c9-334243-hs` (inside kept job 334243, c9) COMPLETED exit 0 on the first attempt and was inspected. Head weight quantization error 0.1219 relative L2. Both context128 cases ACCEPTED under the gate at 151,936 output columns (GQAH versus its FP64 control: relative L2 0.00209 / 0.00192, tokens identical, bitwise equal to eager and on repeat, workspace guards intact). Head effect, reported only: the head-quantized control keeps 64/64 leading tokens of the FP16-head control on the primary passage and 47/64 on the held-out passage; logit relative L2 between the two controls 0.170 / 0.123 at the first step. vLLM accepted the quantized head (model memory 1.23 GiB against 1.12 GiB) and its tokens equal GQAH's in all 64 positions. One held-out cell each, smoke only, not the result: GQAH 271.5 ms, V 238.9 ms, VH 180.8 ms. Because c9 had under an hour left, the complete predeclared run (contexts 128 and 2048) is queued inside kept job 334245 on c10 behind the ORT long-context run; c9's remaining time runs the same cells at context128 only, as a second-node replication decided before any full result.

### E031 result — both arms pass on every node; vLLM with the project's 4-bit head is 23–24% faster than stock vLLM (2026-11-10, c10, c7, c9)

Runs, all exit 0 inside kept allocations, no failed attempt: `head-full-c10-334245-hf` (the predeclared complete run, contexts 128 and 2048, 32 cells), `head-full-c7-334241-hf` (the same on a second node, started when c7 became free, before any full result was read), `head-full-c9-334243-hf128` (context128 only, 16 cells). 80 cells, 1600 timed requests. `results/e031_20261110/`; logit archives and the head checkpoint stay on BeeGFS (`e031-all.sha256`, `model-gptq-head.sha256`).

Correctness of the head-quantized model (gate): all four cases ACCEPTED on c10 and on c7 with identical values, and both context128 cases on c9. GQAH versus its FP64 control: relative L2 0.00192–0.00233 against the 0.003 bound, tokens identical in every case (near-tie rule unused), bitwise equal to eager and on repeat, workspace guards intact at 151,936 output columns. vLLM with the head checkpoint returned GQAH's 64 tokens in every request; stock vLLM returned GQA's.

Decode medians, ranges over two passages and two reversed series:

| Node | Context | GQA ms | GQAH ms | vLLM ms | vLLM + 4-bit head ms | GQAH below GQA | VH below V | GQAH versus V | GQAH versus VH |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| c10 | 128 | 326.8–326.9 | 271.4–271.5 | 237.4–238.6 | 180.7–180.8 | 17.0% | 23.9–24.3% | 13.7–14.3% slower | 50.1–50.2% slower |
| c10 | 2048 | 375.4–375.6 | 320.2–320.3 | 248.6–249.2 | 190.9–191.1 | 14.7% | 23.1–23.4% | 28.5–28.8% slower | 67.6–67.7% slower |
| c7 | 128 | 325.3–325.4 | 269.8 | 237.8–238.3 | 180.1–180.4 | 17.1% | 24.1–24.4% | 13.2–13.5% slower | 49.6–49.8% slower |
| c7 | 2048 | 373.4–374.0 | 318.1–318.8 | 247.6–248.1 | 190.2–190.8 | 14.8% | 23.1–23.3% | 28.3–28.7% slower | 67.1–67.4% slower |
| c9 | 128 | 326.7–326.8 | 271.3–271.4 | 237.6–238.2 | 180.7 | 17.0% | 23.9–24.1% | 13.9–14.2% slower | 50.1% slower |

- Criterion (≥5% in every cell of a context): **passes for both arms at both contexts on every node.** The saving is 0.88 ms per step in our path and 0.92 ms in vLLM (calculated from the medians), somewhat more than the 0.6–0.8 ms expected.
- vLLM with the project-quantized head decodes at 2.87 ms per step at context128 and 3.03 ms at 2048. On the same node (c10) ONNX Runtime INT4 measured 185.1–185.4 ms and 191.6–192.2 ms earlier the same day (E028): vLLM+head is 2.4–2.5% lower at context128 and 0.3–0.6% lower at 2048 — a tie within the 5% rule, from separate jobs, with different quantizers; it is not a claim of beating ORT. llama.cpp Q4_K_M (208.5/227.5 ms) was measured on c5 only.
- Our own path with the 4-bit head is still slower than stock vLLM (13–29%) and than vLLM+head (50–68%); the E030 gap (merged projections, fused small kernels) is untouched.

Effect on outputs (reported, not gated, NOT a quality evaluation): head weight quantization error 0.1219 relative L2. Against the FP16-head control, the head-quantized control's logits differ by relative L2 0.123–0.170 at the first step; all 64 tokens are kept in three of four cases (both passages at context2048, primary at 128) and 47/64 in one (held-out at 128). This is a large logit change compared with the 0.002 runtime rounding the gate bounds; whether generation quality suffers is unmeasured. For scale only: the 196 block projections were already quantized with weight error 0.118–0.181 in every earlier experiment.

Limits: one model, batch 1, 64 tokens, two passages, RTX 5060 Ti; no perplexity or task metric; the vLLM arm relies on vLLM's own acceptance of `lm_head: true` and its unpatched Marlin; the embedding table stays FP16, so model memory rises by the head codes (vLLM reported 1.23 against 1.12 GiB). Decision: supported decode improvement for both runtimes, conditional on an output-quality evaluation that has not been done. Not a repository default.

## E030 — Where the per-step gap to vLLM lies (diagnostic)

Predeclared 2026-11-10 after the E027 result, with the user's agreement ("alright") to the proposed next step. Question: on identical weights vLLM spends 3.8–3.9 ms per decode step and our GQA path 5.2–6.0 ms; which GPU kernels account for the difference? No path is changed and no speedup is claimed.

Method: PyTorch profiler (CUPTI kernel records), same node, contexts 128 and 2048, primary passage. For each stack, after warm requests, one profiled 2-token request and one profiled 34-token request; per-kernel device time and launch count per decode step = (34-token − 2-token) / 32, which removes prefill. Ours: GQA with graph replay (`scripts/e030/gprofile.py`). vLLM: same checkpoint as E027 with its engine run in-process (`VLLM_ENABLE_V1_MULTIPROCESSING=0`) so the profiler sees its kernels; its tokens must equal ours (`vprofile.py`). Reported: total device time per step and the per-kernel table for both, grouped afterwards by inspection of kernel names into Marlin, output head, attention, and the rest.

Limits fixed now: profiler overhead perturbs wall time, so only device kernel durations are used and they need not sum to the E027 wall-clock steps; device time excludes host and idle gaps, so a gap that is host-side will show up as "unexplained" rather than be located; in-process vLLM is not the configuration timed in E027; kernel names may not identify every operation; one passage, one node. The outcome selects at most one next experiment and is not itself an optimization.

Execution notes: backup job 334252 on c51 FAILED exit 1 in our side of the profile before any profiling: the E017 request function only accepts 8 or 64 generated tokens, and the scripts asked for 2 and 34. Changed before any profile existed: both stacks now use an 8-token and a 64-token request and divide the difference by 56 steps. The copy queued behind the vLLM run on c9 was removed unstarted; the allocation on c51 is kept and the profile rerun inside it.

### E030 result — the gap is located: merged projections and fused small operations; the FP16 output head is the largest remaining item in both stacks (2026-11-10, c51)

Rerun `profile-c51-334252-p2` inside kept job 334252, exit 0; vLLM ran in-process and returned our tokens. `results/e030_20261110/` (`prof/ours.json`, `prof/vllm.json`). Device kernel time per decode step, (64-token − 8-token)/56, primary passage; grouping by kernel name (Marlin template, the FP16 `gemvx` output-head kernel, everything else):

| Per decode step | Ours 128 | vLLM 128 | Ours 2048 | vLLM 2048 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Total device time | 5109 µs | 3733 µs | 5846 µs | 3820 µs |
| Kernel launches | 1461 | 389 | 1461 | 389 |
| Marlin projections | 2405 µs, 196 calls | 2019 µs, 112 calls | 2396 µs, 196 calls | 2018 µs, 112 calls |
| FP16 output head (one GEMV) | 1169 µs | 1169 µs | 1169 µs | 1169 µs |
| Everything else | 1535 µs, 1264 launches | 545 µs, 276 launches | 2281 µs, 1264 launches | 633 µs, 276 launches |

Observations:
- Device totals agree with the E027 wall-clock steps (ours 5.2/6.0 ms, vLLM 3.8/3.9 ms), so in both stacks the decode step is device-bound and the gap is on the device, not in host overhead.
- Gap at context128: 1376 µs = 386 µs in Marlin + 990 µs in everything else. At context2048: 2026 µs = 378 µs + 1648 µs.
- Marlin: vLLM makes 112 calls per step against our 196. Its model definition merges the query/key/value projections into one matrix and the two MLP input projections into one, so each block issues 4 calls instead of 7 (documented vLLM structure, consistent with the count; the per-call cause of the 0.38 ms was not isolated further).
- Everything else: we launch 1264 small PyTorch kernels per step (elementwise add/mul, copies, normalization pieces, concatenation, index copies, our attention's two GEMV calls and softmax); vLLM launches 276, mostly fused Triton kernels from its compiled backbone (fused add+RMS-norm, fused rotary/positional arithmetic, fused SiLU·mul) plus two FlashAttention kernels and one cache-write kernel per block. At context2048 our attention path (two GEMV kernels 585 µs plus 558 µs of copies) costs more than FlashAttention's two kernels (380 µs).
- The FP16 output head costs the same 1169 µs in both stacks. It is 31% of vLLM's step and 23% of ours at context128, and the largest single kernel in either.

Interpretation (hypotheses, not measurements): (a) reaching vLLM's speed in our path would need merged projections and fused elementwise/normalization kernels, i.e. reimplementing what vLLM's model code and compiler already provide; (b) the output head is the one large cost vLLM has not removed. llama.cpp and ONNX Runtime evidently quantize that matrix and are 0.5–0.9 ms per step faster than vLLM (E028 versus E027, different nodes); whether quantizing the head with Marlin recovers a similar amount, and what it does to outputs, is untested.

Limits: one passage, one node, profiler attached, in-process vLLM (not the timed configuration), grouping by kernel-name inspection, no host-side attribution needed since totals match. The first attempt's failure is recorded above. Decision: no optimization follows automatically; the supported next experiment is a Marlin-quantized output head, which changes model outputs and therefore needs its own numerical reference and an output-agreement report.

## E029 — Our decode paths at contexts beyond 2048

Predeclared 2026-11-10 at the user's request to test context lengths beyond 2048. No code change: E026's tooling and variants (GR, GQ, GRA, GQA) at contexts 4096, 8192 and 16384, both passages, two reversed series, 3 warmups + 20 requests per cell. The passages are the same two texts repeated to the required token count, as at every earlier context. Each case is validated first by E026's `att.py validate` under E021's gate (third-gate disclosure applies); a context whose validation is rejected or cannot run (memory, time) is reported as such and not timed, with no gate edit.

Hypotheses, from the E026 measurements (calculations, not results): (a) GQA stays ≥5% below GRA in every cell at each context, with the percentage shrinking as attention grows — linear extrapolation gives roughly 38%, 34% and 27% at 4096, 8192 and 16384; (b) GQA's decode step grows about 0.41 ms per 1,000 context tokens. Reported: decode medians and per-step times for all four variants, GQA versus GRA/GR/GQ, end-to-end and prefill share, peak allocated memory, and the departure from the linear projection. Prefill is unchanged eager code and is expected to dominate end-to-end time at these contexts; that is reported, not optimized.

Limits fixed now: one model (trained limit 32,768 positions), batch 1, 64 generated tokens, repeated-passage prompts, one node; no sanitizer coverage at these prefill sizes. `scripts/e028/submit.sh long`. Smoke (context4096 validation + one GQA cell) inspected before the dependent full job.

Execution notes: smoke 334241 on c7 FAILED exit 1 in validation at context4096 before any timing: the E017 operator wrapper asserts at most 2048 activation rows per call, and a 4096-token prefill exceeds it. Deviation from "no code change", recorded before any result: `scripts/e028/long.py` runs E026's `att.py` with that assertion bound raised to 16384 (the wrapper's workspace size does not depend on the bound above 64 rows; decode is one row and unaffected). Whether Marlin's existing dispatch is correct at these prefill sizes is exactly what the unchanged E021 gate then tests per context. At the user's instruction not to release GPUs, the finished job's allocation is kept (`scripts/e028/inalloc.sh`, batch shell paused) and later stages run inside it as tagged directories.

### E029 result — both hypotheses hold to 8192; the step grows faster than linear at 16384; prefill dominates (2026-11-10, c7)

Smoke 334241 failed (recorded above); rerun `long-smoke-c7-334241-s2` and full `long-full-c7-334241-full` inside kept job 334241, exit 0, 48 cells, 960 timed requests; `results/e029_20261110/`. Validation: all six cases ACCEPTED under the unchanged gate with the raised row bound (GQA versus FP64 control relative L2 0.00208–0.00224, tokens identical, workspace guards intact), so Marlin's existing dispatch is correct for prefills of 4096–16384 rows in this test.

| Context | GR ms | GQ ms | GRA ms | GQA ms | GQA below GRA | GQA below GQ | GQA below GR | GQA ms per step | GQA end-to-end ms | Peak allocated GR / GQA GiB |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 4096 | 1210.5–1210.8 | 943.8–944.0 | 698.6–698.7 | 426.8–427.0 | 38.9% | 54.8% | 64.7% | 6.78 | 921–922 | 3.39 / 1.62 |
| 8192 | 1875.1–1876.1 | 1602.1–1602.3 | 801.6–801.7 | 540.7–540.8 | 32.5% | 66.3% | 71.2% | 8.58 | 1927–1928 | 3.84 / 2.07 |
| 16384 | 3149.1–3152.2 | 2878.8–2881.3 | 1063.6–1063.7 | 795.8–796.1 | 25.2% | 72.3–72.4% | 74.7–74.8% | 12.64 | 5053 | 4.95 / 3.17 |

- (a) holds: GQA is ≥5% below GRA in every cell; 38.9%, 32.5%, 25.2% against the projected 38%, 34%, 27%. The Marlin saving stays 4.1–4.3 ms per step in absolute terms while the common cost grows.
- (b) holds to 8192 and not beyond: projected 6.78 / 8.46 / 11.82 ms per step, measured 6.78 / 8.58 / 12.64. Between 8192 and 16384 the slope is 0.50 ms per 1,000 tokens, not 0.41; the cause was not profiled.
- Prefill is the unchanged eager code and dominates: time to first token 0.49 s, 1.39 s and 4.26 s for GQA, i.e. 54%, 72% and 84% of the 64-token request.
- Peak allocated memory stays 1.8 GiB lower with Marlin at every context.

Limits as predeclared; in addition, the peak-memory and first-token figures are from the primary-passage series-1 files. Compared with production stacks at these contexts the path falls further behind (E027/E028 extensions).

## E028 — Our decode path versus llama.cpp and ONNX Runtime GenAI

Predeclared 2026-11-10 at the user's request to queue separate jobs for other state-of-the-art inference stacks, alongside E027 (vLLM). Nothing in our paths changes. Two independent comparators, each in its own job and node-local venv, each built from the same pinned base model files:

- llama.cpp via llama-cpp-python built from its PyPI sdist with CUDA, all layers on the GPU. LF = FP16 GGUF from the sdist's own `convert_hf_to_gguf.py`; LQ = llama.cpp's own Q4_K_M quantization of it.
- ONNX Runtime GenAI (CUDA provider) from PyPI. OF = its model builder's FP16 export; OQ = the builder's own INT4 export.

Unlike E027, these stacks cannot load the project's G128 codes: their 4-bit formats and quantizers are their own, so the 4-bit comparison is between complete stacks (format + kernel + runtime), not between runtimes on identical weights. Versions are whatever PyPI serves on the day, recorded by `pip freeze` and the hashed sdist; a rebuilt venv is constrained to the first freeze.

Conditions as E027: batch 1, greedy, 64 tokens, the exact input ids of our validated runs, contexts 128, 512, 2048 × two passages × two reversed series, 3 warmups + 20 requests, one process per cell, GR and GQA re-validated and re-timed in the same job on the same node. External decode time = 63 single-token steps including the stack's sampling call (argmax over raw logits for llama.cpp; `generate_next_token` for ORT), prefill excluded; end-to-end also reported.

Checks: the FP16 variant of each stack must produce our FP16 path's first token in every request or the cell fails; leading-token agreement (of 64) is reported for FP16 and 4-bit variants and not gated, since the 4-bit weights differ. ORT GenAI stops at EOS with no ignore option; a request ending before 64 tokens fails its cell. No output-quality comparison of the three quantizers is made or implied.

Outcome per stack and context, fixed now, GQA versus the stack's 4-bit variant with E027's rule: "faster" (GQA ≥5% below in all four cells), "slower", "comparable" (all within ±5%), otherwise "mixed". Also reported: our GR versus the stack's FP16, and the stack's own 4-bit gain over its FP16.

Not covered: TensorRT-LLM and ExLlamaV2 (engine build / calibration toolchains not attempted), batch sizes above 1, tuning of either stack. `scripts/e028/`. A failed install or build is recorded as a failed attempt of that comparator only. Smokes inspected before dependent full jobs.

Execution notes: llama.cpp smoke 334239 on c7 FAILED exit 1 during the CUDA build of llama-cpp-python 0.3.36, before any model conversion or measurement: `-march=native` made GCC 12.4 emit AVX-VNNI/AVX512-BF16 instructions the node's assembler rejects. The build now passes `-DGGML_NATIVE=OFF` (portable host ISA; all model layers run on the GPU). Both submit scripts now rebuild a venv unless a completed-install marker exists, so a failed install cannot be mistaken for a usable environment.

ONNX Runtime smoke 334245 on c10 FAILED exit 1 at the FP16 model build, before any measurement: the model builder imports `onnx_ir`, which the PyPI packages did not pull in. `onnx-ir` is now installed explicitly; the environment and its freeze were discarded and rebuilt inside the kept allocation.

ONNX Runtime rerun `ort-smoke-c10-334245-s2` (inside kept job 334245) COMPLETED exit 0 and was inspected: onnxruntime-genai-cuda 0.19.0 / onnxruntime-gpu 1.31.1; FP16 export 3.0 GB, INT4 export 872 MB (smaller than the block projections plus an FP16 embedding would be, so the builder evidently also quantizes the tied embedding/output head — to be confirmed from the model files). One context128 held-out cell each, smoke only, not the result: GQA 326.4 ms, OF 531.1 ms, OQ 185.0 ms; leading tokens agreeing with ours 46/64 (FP16) and 29/64 (INT4). Before the full job, and before any full result, the token read-back in `obench.py` was moved inside the timed region so that work still queued on the GPU cannot be left out of the decode time; the smoke numbers above were taken without that and are not used.

llama.cpp smoke 334244 on c5 COMPLETED exit 0 and was inspected: llama-cpp-python 0.3.36 built with CUDA; Q4_K_M file 934.7 MiB at 5.08 bits per weight over all tensors, i.e. llama.cpp also quantizes the embedding/output matrix. One context128 held-out cell each, smoke only: GQA 326.1 ms, LF 528.0 ms, LQ 208.3 ms; leading tokens agreeing with ours 46/64 (FP16) and 28/64 (Q4_K_M). Allocation kept; full job started inside it.

### E028 result, ONNX Runtime GenAI — our path is slower at every context (2026-11-10, c10)

Full run `ort-full-c10-334245-full` inside kept job 334245, exit 0, 48 cells, 960 timed requests; `results/e028_20261110/` (logit archives stay on BeeGFS, hashes in `ort-all.sha256`). GR/GQA validation accepted in all six cases. Decode medians, ranges over two passages and two reversed series:

| Context | GR ms | GQA ms | ORT FP16 ms | ORT INT4 ms | GQA versus ORT INT4 | GR versus ORT FP16 | ORT INT4 below its FP16 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 128 | 616.0–616.7 | 326.8–327.6 | 531.0–531.7 | 185.2–185.4 | 76.5–76.7% slower | 15.9–16.1% slower | 65.1–65.2% |
| 512 | 673.5–673.6 | 336.1–336.7 | 532.5–532.7 | 185.1–185.3 | 81.4–81.9% slower | 26.4–26.5% slower | 65.2–65.3% |
| 2048 | 905.2–905.3 | 376.1–376.2 | 539.3–540.1 | 191.6–192.2 | 95.8–96.3% slower | 67.6–67.8% slower | 64.4–64.5% |

Predeclared outcome: **"slower" at all three contexts.** ORT's INT4 path decodes in 2.9–3.0 ms per step and is nearly flat in context; ours takes 5.2–6.0 ms. ORT's FP16 path also beats our graph FP16 and is flat in context, so its attention path does not have the cost ours had. Our 4-bit path remains faster than ORT's FP16 (38–30%). End-to-end (64 tokens): GQA 342/379/574 ms versus ORT INT4 205/235/372 ms. Token agreement with ours: primary 64/64 for both ORT variants at every context; held-out 46/64 (FP16) and 29/64 (INT4) at context128 and 64/64 at 512 and 2048; the FP16 first-token check passed in every request. The timed token read-back did not change the smoke value (185 ms).

Limits: ORT's INT4 weights come from its own quantizer and include a quantized embedding/output matrix, so this is stack against stack, not kernel against kernel; no output-quality comparison; batch 1; one node; versions as recorded in `ort-freeze.txt`. Why ORT's step is this short (kernel, fused graph, quantized head) was not profiled and must not be attributed.
