## E027 — Our decode path versus vLLM on the same quantized weights

Predeclared 2026-11-10 at the user's request ("go") after the question whether the result is faster than or comparable to a state-of-the-art inference stack. Every earlier full-model comparison used Hugging Face Transformers FP16 as the baseline. This experiment adds one external comparator and changes nothing in our paths.

Comparator V: vLLM v0.33.0 (the pinned, hashed wheel that is also the source of our Marlin build) installed with its PyPI dependencies in a separate node-local venv, offline `LLM.generate`, its own Qwen2 implementation, scheduler and unmodified Marlin kernel (without the E006 barrier). It loads the SAME project G128 codes and FP16 scales, written by `scripts/e027/convert.py` as an AutoGPTQ-format checkpoint (the project packing is already that layout; no requantization; embedding/head and biases copied). Settings: FP16, batch 1, greedy, exactly 64 tokens with EOS ignored, the exact input ids of our validated runs, prefix caching disabled, model generation defaults ignored, no detokenization.

Our variants, unchanged from E026: GR (graph FP16), GQ (graph Marlin), GQA (graph Marlin + direct decode attention), each validated in the same job by E026's `att.py validate` under E021's gate (third-gate disclosure applies).

Metric: vLLM exposes no synchronized per-phase timer equal to ours, so each V sample is a 1-token request followed by a 64-token request; V decode = their wall-time difference (63 decode steps including vLLM's per-step engine work), median of 20 after 3 warmups. Ours is the usual 63-forward decode median. End-to-end 64-token wall time is reported for both. This difference in measurement method is a stated limit.

Checks on V before its timing is used: the converter decodes every checkpoint tensor the AutoGPTQ way and requires relative L2 < 0.5 against the original weight for all 196 layers (a wrong axis or nibble order gives > 1); V's first generated token must equal our Marlin path's in every request or the cell fails; the number of leading tokens that agree (of 64) is reported, not gated, because vLLM's attention and kernel build round differently.

Hypothesis: GQA's decode median is at least 5% below V's in every (passage, series) cell at each context. Outcome per context, fixed now: "faster" if GQA is ≥5% below V in all four cells; "slower" if V is ≥5% below GQA in all four; "comparable" if all four are within ±5%; otherwise "mixed". All outcomes are reported. Contexts 128, 512, 2048 × primary/held-out × two reversed series, one process per cell, one node for the whole comparison.

Not covered and not to be claimed: vLLM's throughput under concurrent requests (its design point), other stacks (llama.cpp, TensorRT-LLM), vLLM with FP16 weights, any tuning of vLLM. vLLM runs its engine in a child process; the launcher still starts one rank. `scripts/e027/`. Smoke (venv build, conversion, context128 validation, one GQA cell, one V cell) inspected before the dependent full job.

Execution notes: smoke 334232 on c5 FAILED exit 2 after 43 s, before any install or measurement: the pinned wheel is under the first E017 root (`csc548-w4a16-e017-20261109/external/`), not the r2 root the script named. The script now uses that path and checks the wheel's recorded SHA256.

Smoke 334243 on c9 (after cancelled-unstarted 334242, resubmitted with an install marker): install, conversion (196 layers, quantization relative L2 0.118–0.181 against the original weights), context128 validation and the GQA cell (326.3 ms) completed; the V cell FAILED at engine start, before any request. vLLM loaded the checkpoint with its Marlin kernel and FlashAttention 2 and compiled its backbone, then its start-up sampler check tried to JIT-build FlashInfer's top-k/top-p sampling kernel, which needs `ninja` (absent on the node). Greedy decoding does not use that sampler; it is now disabled with vLLM's own switch `VLLM_USE_FLASHINFER_SAMPLER=0` (vLLM then samples with PyTorch). Observed from the log and added to the comparator description: vLLM 0.33.0 runs its model under `torch.compile`. The original checkpoint stores BF16; vLLM casts the unquantized tensors to FP16 as our Transformers path does. The allocation is kept and the smoke rerun inside it.

Rerun `smoke-c9-334243-s2` (inside kept job 334243) COMPLETED exit 0 and was inspected: vLLM starts, reports CUDA-graph capture and a compiled backbone, and returns all 64 tokens identical to our Marlin path. One context128 held-out cell, smoke only: GQA 326.3 ms, V 237.9 ms. Full job started inside the kept allocation.

### E027 result — on identical weights, vLLM decodes faster than our path at every context (2026-11-10, c9)

Full run `full-c9-334243-full` inside kept job 334243, exit 0, 48 cells, 960 timed requests; `results/e027_20261110/` (logit archives and the converted checkpoint stay on BeeGFS, hashes in `e027-all.sha256` and `model-gptq.sha256`). GR/GQ/GQA validation accepted in all six cases. vLLM returned all 64 tokens identical to our Marlin path in every request of every cell. Decode medians, ranges over two passages and two reversed series:

| Context | GR ms | GQ ms | GQA ms | vLLM ms | GQA versus vLLM | vLLM below GR | vLLM 1-token request ms | End-to-end GQA / vLLM ms |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 128 | 616.0–616.1 | 344.1–344.2 | 326.8–327.0 | 238.1–238.8 | 36.9–37.3% slower | 61.2–61.3% | 12.3 | 342 / 250–251 |
| 512 | 672.6–673.4 | 402.0–402.2 | 335.8–336.0 | 240.8–241.1 | 39.4–39.5% slower | 64.2% | 36.3–36.4 | 379 / 277 |
| 2048 | 903.3–905.0 | 638.3–640.1 | 375.5–376.0 | 248.1–248.9 | 50.9–51.4% slower | 72.4–72.6% | 143.2 | 574 / 391–392 |

Predeclared outcome: **"slower" at all three contexts**; the hypothesis (GQA ≥5% below vLLM) is rejected. vLLM takes 3.8–3.9 ms per decode step against our 5.2–6.0 ms, with the same codes, scales and upstream Marlin kernel family, so the difference lies outside the quantized weights; where exactly (its compiled backbone, FlashAttention, CUDA graphs, sampler, or its kernel build) was not profiled and must not be attributed. Its prompt processing is also shorter (1-token request 143 ms at context2048 against our roughly 197 ms prefill). Without our direct attention (GQ) the deficit is 44% at context128 and 157% at 2048.

Limits: vLLM decode is a difference of two wall times, ours a synchronized phase timer; vLLM's Marlin lacks the E006 barrier; its engine runs in a child process; batch 1 only, which is not vLLM's design point and still favours it here; one node; `RequestOutput.metrics` was empty in offline mode. vLLM FP16 was not run.

Extension, as predeclared under E028: vLLM at contexts 4096 and 8192 in the remaining time of the kept c9 allocation (16384 does not fit its time limit there).

Long-context extension result (`full-c9-334243-long`, inside kept job 334243, exit 0, 32 cells; our variants validated through `long.py`, vLLM tokens 64/64 equal to ours in every request):

| Context | GR ms | GQ ms | GQA ms | vLLM ms | GQA versus vLLM | vLLM 1-token request ms | End-to-end GQA / vLLM ms |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 4096 | 1219.5–1219.8 | 953.4–953.6 | 429.5–429.6 | 266.3–266.5 | 61.2–61.3% slower | 302.6–304.4 | 928–929 / 569–571 |
| 8192 | 1891.2–1891.5 | 1617.4–1617.9 | 544.5–544.7 | 284.1–284.8 | 91.2–91.7% slower | 682.7–683.3 | 1942–1944 / 967 |

Outcome "slower" at both contexts, and the deficit widens with context: our step grows from 6.8 to 8.6 ms while vLLM's grows from 4.2 to 4.5 ms. Our prefill is also about 500 ms at 4096 and 1400 ms at 8192 against vLLM's 303 and 683 ms for a whole 1-token request. Context 16384 was not run for vLLM (time limit of that allocation).

## E026 — Single-query grouped decode attention under graph replay

Predeclared 2026-11-10 from the E025 result, under the user's standing instruction to continue toward robust optimizations while away. One main change: for decode steps only (one query token), attention is computed as `softmax(scale·q·Kᵀ + mask)·V` directly on the 2 stored key/value heads with FP32 scores and one final rounding to FP16, instead of Transformers' SDPA call after expanding keys/values to 12 heads. Prefill and every multi-token call keep the stock path. Static shapes and addresses are unchanged, so E023's graph capture applies. No kernel, quantization, Marlin, tolerance or cache change. This is an inference-side change common to FP16 and Marlin; it is not a W4A16 kernel result, and its gain must be reported against an FP16 path using the same attention.

Hypothesis: graph-Marlin with the new attention (GQA) lowers the decode median by ≥5% versus graph-Marlin with stock attention (GQ) in every (passage, series) cell of a context. Expected from E025 (calculation, not a result): a gain at long context and a possible regression at context128, where the stock kernel is already cheap and the new path adds several small kernels; a regression is reported as such, and any context-dependent selection would be a separate experiment.

Variants: GR, GQ (stock attention), GRA, GQA (new attention), all graph-replayed. Contexts 128, 512, 2048 × primary/held-out, c-node chosen by availability with the hashed runtime archive.

Validation before timing, `scripts/e026/att.py validate`: dual-module process, each case at 64 tokens. The reference stays E021's FP64-projection control with STOCK attention. GQA must pass E021's gate against it unchanged (tokens with the near-tie rule, relative L2 ≤ 0.003, finite, workspace, state), must equal the eager new-attention Marlin run bitwise, and must repeat bitwise. Because the new attention rounds differently from the stock kernel, this gate now also absorbs attention rounding; failing it is a rejection with no gate edit and no timing. The function itself is checked against PyTorch's attention definition in FP32 at install. E021's disclosure (third gate, post hoc) applies.

Timing after inspected acceptance: one process per cell, 3 warmups + 20 requests, two fully reversed series, each variant checked against its own validated tokens and bitwise logits. Reported: GQA versus GQ (criterion), GRA versus GR, GQA versus GRA (the Marlin gain with attention equalized), end-to-end, spreads. `scripts/e026/`. Smoke (context128 validation + one timed cell) inspected before the dependent full job.

Execution notes recorded 2026-11-10 before any full-job result was read: smoke 334148 FAILED exit 1 at install because the self-check called PyTorch's SDPA on CPU, whose math backend this runtime disables; the check now writes the attention definition out explicitly (committed). Smoke 334149 on c7 COMPLETED and inspected (both context128 cases accepted). Full job 334151 (contexts 128, 512, 2048) is running. Because the submitting laptop's VPN is about to drop, a second full job 334160 for contexts 256 and 1024 is queued behind it with `afterok`, extending coverage to E024's five contexts under the same inspected smoke and the same criteria; both jobs run unattended and their results stay on BeeGFS until retrieved under a later holding job.

### E026 result — passes at contexts 256–2048; context128 is 4.9–5.1% and not a robust pass (2026-11-10, c7, UUID GPU-29e9569a-8a7a-cd23-5443-bd0b72ca241a, driver 610.57.04)

Attempts: smokes 334148 (failed) and 334149 as noted above; full 334151 (contexts 128, 512, 2048) and 334160 (256, 1024) COMPLETED exit 0 unattended. No failed full job. Identity checks pass. 336 files listed in `e026-all.sha256`, non-tensor files hash-verified, `results/e026_20261110/`.

Validation, 10 cases at 64 tokens against the stock-attention FP64-projection control: all accepted. GQA's tokens equal the control's in every case (the near-tie rule was not used); relative L2 0.00200–0.00252 against the 0.003 bound, versus 0.00192–0.00259 for stock-attention GQ in the same runs, so the new attention does not move Marlin measurably farther from the control. GQA is bitwise equal to its eager counterpart and repeatable. The attention change by itself moves logits by relative L2 0.0017–0.0020 (GQA versus GQ), the same size as the projection rounding. E021's disclosure applies.

Timing, 80 cells, 1600 timed requests; ranges over both passages and both reversed series, decode medians of 20:

| Context | GR ms | GQ ms | GRA ms | GQA ms | GQA below GQ (criterion) | GRA below GR | GQA below GRA | GQA below GR |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 128 | 614.4–615.0 | 342.4–343.0 | 596.3–596.8 | 325.2–325.8 | 5.0% | 2.9–3.0% | 45.4–45.5% | 47.0–47.1% |
| 256 | 632.7–633.5 | 362.7–363.6 | 600.2–600.8 | 328.7–329.3 | 9.4% | 5.1–5.2% | 45.2% | 48.0–48.1% |
| 512 | 670.6–671.3 | 399.7–400.6 | 606.9–606.9 | 334.2–334.8 | 16.4% | 9.5–9.6% | 44.8–44.9% | 50.1–50.2% |
| 1024 | 749.4–750.3 | 476.1–477.1 | 627.2–627.2 | 350.9–351.4 | 26.3–26.4% | 16.3–16.4% | 44.0–44.1% | 53.2% |
| 2048 | 900.0–900.1 | 633.8–634.0 | 650.0–650.1 | 373.9–374.0 | 41.0% | 27.8% | 42.5% | 58.5% |

- Criterion (GQA ≥5% below GQ in every cell of a context): **passes at all five contexts**. At context128 the margin is 5.004–5.031%, i.e. at the threshold; it should be described as "about 5%", not as a clear pass. The gain grows with context to 41.0% at 2048.
- The expected short-context regression did not occur: the direct path is slightly faster even where the stock kernel was cheap.
- The change is not specific to Marlin: FP16 gains 2.9% at context128 and 27.8% at 2048 from it. With attention equalized, Marlin's advantage over FP16 is 45.5% at context128 and 42.5% at 2048, so the Marlin gain no longer fades with context; previously it fell to 29.6%.
- Decode time now grows only from 325 to 374 ms between context128 and 2048 (5.2 to 5.9 ms per step).
- Passage and series differences are under 0.2%; GQA's largest min–max spread over 20 requests is 1.04 ms.
- End-to-end at context2048 is 570 ms for GQA, of which roughly 196 ms is the unchanged eager prefill, now about a third of the request.

Limits: contexts 256, 512 and 1024 measured on c7 only, one model, batch 1, 64 tokens; reference tokens and logits are each variant's own validated output; the custom attention handles only batch 1 with an explicit mask and one query token; no sanitizer or trace under replay; third-gate acceptance. PyTorch supplies the matmul/softmax kernels; ours is the decode-path restructuring and its validation.

Second-node replication (added the same day, after the c7 result was read; not in the predeclaration): smoke 334175 and full 334177 on c9 (UUID GPU-02862ae3-8b7f-fb81-413c-791b5874af21, driver 610.57.04, hashed runtime archive), contexts 128 and 2048, both passages, two series, 32 cells, both COMPLETED exit 0. All four validation cases accepted with values identical to c7's. Context2048 replicates: GQA 41.1–41.3% below GQ, 42.3–42.4% below GRA, 58.4–58.5% below GR. **Context128 does not pass on c9:** GQA is 4.90%, 4.90%, 5.02% and 5.06% below GQ in its four cells, so two cells miss 5%. Against GRA and GR the context128 gains replicate (45.3% and 46.9%). `e026-c9-all.sha256` lists the retrieved files.

Decision: supported improvement at contexts 256–2048 on c7 and at 2048 on both nodes. At context128 the gain over stock-attention graph-Marlin is 4.9–5.1% across two nodes: real and repeatable in sign, but not a robust pass of the 5% criterion, and it must not be claimed as one. Not made a repository default; any report must present it as an inference-path change that benefits FP16 too and quote Marlin's gain against GRA, not only against stock-attention FP16.

## E025 — Decode-step device time by operation (diagnostic)

Predeclared 2026-11-10 from `fe36faf`, under the user's standing instruction to continue toward robust optimizations while away. Diagnostic only: no optimization, kernel or model-path change, no timing claim.

Motivation (E024 calculation): graph-Marlin's decode step is 5.44 ms at context128 and 10.06 ms at context2048; FP16's is 4.4–4.5 ms higher at every context. So both paths share a cost of about 2.4 µs per context token per step, and at context128 about 3 ms of the Marlin step is neither that nor (by E022's bound) projections. Question: which operations carry (i) the context-proportional device time and (ii) the remaining short-context device time?

Method, `scripts/e025/opprofile.py`, c7, one Q process, unchanged eager E017 runner: per context 128 and 2048, one warm 8-token request, then one 8-token request under `torch.profiler` (CPU+CUDA activities, E017 markers). Device kernel time for kernels starting inside the decode span is attributed to the launching operator and reported per decode step, with the 2048−128 difference per operator. Only device time is used; profiler overhead makes host times invalid. Profiled-request tokens must equal the unprofiled request's.

Reading: the operator family with the largest 2048−128 device-time difference is the candidate for one next predeclared optimization; if no family carries at least half of the difference, the result is recorded as diffuse and no optimization is proposed from it. Kernel durations under an eager profiler are not graph-replay latencies; sums are compared with E024's step times as a consistency check only. Smoke: context128 alone, inspected before the dependent full job.

### E025 result — attention carries the context-proportional time; the FP16 output head is the largest fixed non-Marlin item (2026-11-10, c9, UUID GPU-02862ae3-8b7f-fb81-413c-791b5874af21, driver 610.57.04)

Attempts: smoke 334140 cancelled unstarted (c7 was taken by another user; job moved to idle c9 with the hashed E024 runtime archive unpacked to the identical path). Smokes 334142 (my path edit broke the E017 root), 334143 (script named `profile.py` shadowed the standard library) and 334144 (decode span counted twice because the profiler mirrors annotations on the device timeline) FAILED exit 1 before producing any result; each fixed and committed. Smoke 334145 COMPLETED and inspected; full 334147 COMPLETED exit 0. 60 files hash-verified, `results/e025_20261110/`. Profiled-request tokens equal the unprofiled request's; identity checks pass.

| Device time per eager Marlin decode step, µs | context128 | context2048 | difference |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Marlin kernels (196 projections) | 2443 | 2490 | 47 |
| FP16 output head GEMV (`aten::mm`, 151936×1536, not quantized) | 1169 | 1175 | 6 |
| Attention kernel (`fmha_cutlassF`, memory-efficient SDPA) | 469 | 3996 | 3527 |
| `aten::copy_` (includes key/value head expansion) | 366 | 1420 | 1055 |
| All other kernels (norms, RoPE, residuals, activation, cache update, argmax) | 1084 | 1100 | 16 |
| Total | 5530 | 10181 | 4651 |

Consistency: the totals, 5.53 and 10.18 ms, agree with E024's graph-replayed Marlin step times of 5.44 and 10.06 ms on c7, so under graph replay the step is essentially its device time.

Predeclared reading: the attention operator carries 78% of the 2048−128 difference (≥ half), and the key/value expansion copy another 23%; everything else is context-independent within 0.1 ms. The candidate for one next optimization is therefore the single-query decode attention path. Interpretation (not measured further): the memory-efficient SDPA kernel serves one query token against 2112 cached keys at about 143 µs per layer, far above the arithmetic involved, and the explicit mask forces Transformers to materialize 12 key/value heads from 2.

Also observed, not acted on: the unquantized tied output head costs about 1.17 ms per step at both contexts (21% of the short-context step), close to its 467 MB weight traffic at device bandwidth; quantizing it would be a quantization-scope change with model-output consequences and is a separate question. Marlin's 196 projections take about 2.45 ms. The roughly 1.3 ms of small elementwise kernels is spread over many launches.

Limits: one profiled request per context, seven decode steps, eager execution under profiler instrumentation, one node. Kernel durations are not replay latencies.

## E024 — Robustness of the E020/E023 decode gains

Predeclared 2026-11-10 from `cc8a4c0`, requested by the user ("check if these gains are robust enough"). No new mechanism: the four E023 variants (R eager FP16, GR graph FP16, Q eager Marlin, GQ graph Marlin) with unchanged library, integration, graph procedure and E021 gate. What changes is only the conditions under which they are measured.

Threats tested:
- **Other inputs:** contexts 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048 × primary and held-out passages (E020/E023 timed only primary at 128/2048). Each timed variant is checked against its own validated tokens and bitwise-anchored logits, so the context128 held-out case, where R and Marlin legitimately emit different tokens after R's exact tie, can be timed; there the paths generate different text of equal length, disclosed.
- **Repetition:** fresh processes, both series orders reversed across contexts, passages and variants; context128/2048 primary cells repeat E023.
- **Another GPU of the same model:** the same run on a second RTX 5060 Ti node at contexts 128 and 2048, both passages, using the c7 runtime copied byte-for-byte to the identical node-local path, with that node's own full validation first (fresh validation for a changed node/driver).

Validation per node before its timing: dual-module process, every context × passage at 64 tokens, graph-Marlin under E021's gate against the FP64-projection control, bitwise equal to eager Marlin and repeatable (E021 disclosure applies). Timing: 3 warmups + 20 requests per cell, one process per cell; 80 cells on c7, 32 on the second node.

Predeclared robustness criteria, evaluated per node over every (context, passage, series) cell:
- (a) GQ decode median ≥5% lower than both R and GR in every cell;
- (b) the slowest of GQ's 20 requests is faster than the fastest of R's and GR's in every cell;
- (c) on c7, context128/2048 primary medians of each variant within 2% of E023's.
"Robust" means all three hold on c7 and (a),(b) hold on the second node. Any failing cell is reported as the boundary of the claim, with no rerun to replace it. Eager-Marlin-versus-R gains are reported per cell without a criterion (E020 already shows they are below 5% at short context).

Not covered: other models, batch sizes, GPU models, generated lengths, system load from other users, sanitizers under graph replay, model quality. ARC: sbatch+prun, one rank per node; per node an inspected smoke (context128 validation plus one timed cell) before the dependent full job; `scripts/e024/`.

### E024 result — robust under all predeclared criteria (2026-11-10)

Attempts: c7 smoke 334110, runtime pack 334111, c7 full 334112, c52 smoke 334113, c52 full 334114, all COMPLETED exit 0, each smoke inspected before its full job. No failed job. A VPN drop on the submitting laptop interrupted retrieval of 334112 after it finished; its results were unaffected on BeeGFS and were copied out under a 20-minute no-compute holding job 334138. c7: UUID GPU-29e9569a-8a7a-cd23-5443-bd0b72ca241a, driver 610.57.04. c52: UUID GPU-f265b6be-2f35-5a5f-50b8-22bf81ec80b8, driver 615.71.09, runtime unpacked from the hashed c7 archive; binary, source and pip identities pass on both.

Validation: all 10 c7 cases and all 4 c52 cases accepted at 64 tokens; graph-Marlin bitwise equal to eager Marlin and repeatable; relative L2 to the FP64-projection control 0.00192–0.00259, identical values on both nodes; tokens equal the control's everywhere. E021's disclosure applies.

c7, 80 cells, 1600 timed requests. Ranges are over both passages and both series (four cells per context), decode medians of 20:

| Context | R ms | GR ms | Q ms | GQ ms | GQ below R | GQ below GR | Q below R |

| 256 | 644.7–645.1 | 633.4–633.5 | 593.5–596.0 | 362.9–363.5 | 43.6–43.8% | 42.6–42.7% | 7.6–7.9% |
| 512 | 682.3–682.4 | 671.3–671.4 | 589.1–593.1 | 399.9–400.6 | 41.3–41.4% | 40.3–40.4% | 13.1–13.7% |

c52, 32 cells, 640 timed requests:

| Context | R ms | GR ms | Q ms | GQ ms | GQ below R | GQ below GR | Q below R |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 128 | 622.0–622.7 | 610.8–611.0 | 588.5–593.6 | 337.5–338.2 | 45.7–45.7% | 44.6–44.7% | 4.7–5.4% |
| 2048 | 899.9–901.3 | 889.2–890.4 | 636.3–636.6 | 622.9–623.1 | 30.8–30.9% | 29.9–30.0% | 29.3–29.4% |

Criteria:
- (a) GQ ≥5% below both R and GR in every cell: **passes**, 20/20 cells on c7 (minimum 29.56%) and 8/8 on c52 (minimum 29.95%).
- (b) slowest GQ request faster than fastest R/GR request in every cell: **passes**, 28/28.
- (c) c7 context128/2048 primary medians within 2% of E023: **passes**; largest deviation 0.70% (eager Q), graph variants within 0.21%.

Observations:
- Passage has no measurable effect: primary and held-out medians differ by under 0.1% for R, GR and GQ at every context, including context128 held-out where R and Marlin emit different tokens.
- Series order has no measurable effect on R, GR, GQ (≤0.2%); eager Q varies up to about 1% between cells.
- c52 medians are 0.6–1.3% lower than c7 for R and GR and 1.4–1.7% lower for GQ; eager Q ranges from 0.3% higher to 1.8% lower. Gains agree within 0.5 percentage points.
- GQ's largest min–max spread over 20 requests is 0.89 ms; eager Q's is 8.25 ms.
- The gain falls smoothly with context: 45.3% → 43.7% → 41.4% → 37.3% → 30.4% versus R on c7. In absolute terms GQ is 282–285 ms faster than R at contexts 128–1024 and 278 ms faster at 2048, i.e. about 4.4–4.5 ms per step; both paths carry the same context-proportional cost, about 2.4 µs per context token per step (calculation from the medians). That common cost is unattributed.
- Eager Marlin's gain over R rises from 5.5% to 28.9% with context, crossing 5% already at context128 in these cells (E020/E023 measured 3.6–5.4% there): at short context it sits at the threshold and should not be claimed as passing.

Decision: the graph-replayed Marlin decode gain is robust across five contexts, two passages, reversed order, repetition and a second GPU/driver of the same model. Not established: other GPU models, other models, batch sizes, generated lengths, shared-node load, sanitizer coverage under replay. Evidence: `results/e024_20261110/` (non-tensor files hash-verified; `e024-c7-all.sha256`/`e024-c52-all.sha256` also list the anchor logit archives left on BeeGFS under `csc548-w4a16-e024-20261110`).

## E023 — CUDA-graph replay of the Marlin decode step

Predeclared 2026-11-10 from `6f01dc2`, requested by the user ("get a new node and test this out") as the one hypothesis E022 supports. One main change: the per-token decode step of the Marlin model (forward + argmax) is captured once with `torch.cuda.CUDAGraph` and replayed per token, exactly E015's procedure (three side-stream warmups, capture at the first decode position, stable token/position/mask/cache addresses). No kernel, dispatch, tile, model, quantization or tolerance change; corrected E006 A library `e4eca5e3…` and E017 integration unchanged; prefill stays eager. Whether Marlin's external ctypes launches can be captured is unknown and is the first thing the smoke tests; a capture failure is recorded as a concrete negative result.

Hypothesis: graph-replayed Marlin (GQ) lowers the 63-step decode median by ≥5% versus eager Marlin (Q) in both reversed series for a context. Reported without a gate: GQ versus eager FP16 (R) and versus graph-replayed FP16 (GR, the stronger FP16 baseline), TTFT, end-to-end, peak memory, graph setup is inside the untimed first request.

Validation before timing, `scripts/e023/run.py validate`, one dual-module process, contexts 128/2048 × primary/held-out, 64 tokens. GQ must pass E021's gate against the FP64-projection control (tokens, relative L2 ≤ 0.003, finite, workspace, state invariants; same disclosure as E021: third gate, post hoc), and additionally GQ logits must be bitwise equal to eager Q and bitwise repeatable across requests reusing the graph. Per-call operator FP64 probing is not repeated: bitwise equality to the eager path that E021 probed is the link. GR-versus-R bitwise equality is reported.

Timing only after inspected acceptance: separate process per cell, variants R, GR, Q, GQ, primary passage, 3 warmups + 20 requests, series 1 order 128 then 2048 with R,GR,Q,GQ, series 2 fully reversed, 320 timed requests. Each process first reproduces the validated logits for its variant bitwise, and every request passes the unchanged `infer.checked` token/state checks against the E019 R reference. No held-out timing (context128 held-out tokens differ from R at an exact tie). `scripts/e023/analyze.py`.

Not covered: sanitizers under graph replay, complete-model trace, prefill, other models/batch/hardware, tails. ARC: c7 (node-local runtime), sbatch+prun, one rank; 8-token context128 smoke → inspected → validation → inspected → timing.

### E023 result — passes at context128, subthreshold at context2048 (2026-11-10, c7, UUID GPU-29e9569a-8a7a-cd23-5443-bd0b72ca241a, driver 610.57.04)

Attempts: smoke 334103 FAILED exit 1 at import, before GPU work (`validate` resolved to E014's module of the same name); fixed by loading the E021 helpers by path, committed. Smoke 334104, validation 334105 and timing 334106 COMPLETED exit 0, each inspected before the next. Identity checks pass in every job. 147 files hash-verified, `results/e023_20261110/`.

Capture: Marlin's ctypes launches are captured by `torch.cuda.CUDAGraph` without error. Validation, 64 tokens, contexts 128/2048 × both passages: GQ logits are bitwise equal to eager Q and bitwise repeatable in all four cases; E021's gate against the FP64-projection control passes with E021's values (relative L2 0.00198/0.00192/0.00239/0.00213, tokens identical); workspace guards and state invariants hold; GR is bitwise equal to R. Accepted under E021's gate, with E021's disclosure (third gate, post hoc; E017/E019 remain failed).

Timing, 320 timed requests, medians of 20, series 1 / series 2. Every timing process first reproduced its validated logits bitwise; every request passed the unchanged token/state checks.

| Decode ms | R eager FP16 | GR graph FP16 | Q eager Marlin | GQ graph Marlin |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| context128 | 626.2 / 626.8 | 614.3 / 615.0 | 596.1 / 593.3 | 342.4 / 343.0 |
| context2048 | 909.8 / 911.4 | 898.6 / 900.0 | 646.1 / 647.7 | 632.6 / 633.0 |

| GQ decode lower than | Q (criterion) | R | GR |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| context128 | 42.57% / 42.18% | 45.33% / 45.27% | 44.27% / 44.22% |
| context2048 | 2.09% / 2.27% | 30.47% / 30.54% | 29.60% / 29.66% |

End-to-end, GQ versus R: 44.35%/44.30% lower at 128 (357 versus 642 ms), 24.67%/24.79% at 2048 (829 versus 1100 ms). TTFT is unchanged by the graph (prefill stays eager): 14.8 versus 15.8 ms at 128, 196 versus 190 ms at 2048, the same 3% Marlin prefill cost as E020. Peak allocated memory: GQ 1261/1448 MiB versus R 3009/3236 MiB; the graph adds about 65 MiB to either path. GQ decode min–max spread is ≤0.6 ms at 128 and ≤1.7 ms at 2048. Graph capture cost falls in the untimed first request and was not measured.

Decision against the predeclared criterion (GQ ≥5% below eager Q in both series): **passes at context128, does not pass at context2048** (2.1%/2.3%). Graph replay is therefore a supported improvement for short context only; at long context it is neutral-to-slightly-positive and the gain there remains the E020 Marlin-versus-FP16 gain.

Observations and interpretation: the E022 hypothesis is supported at context128, where removing host time cuts the Marlin step from about 9.5 to 5.4 ms while FP16 gains only 1.9% from the same technique. At context2048 the Marlin step stays near 10.0 ms with or without the graph, so it is no longer host-bound there; both paths carry roughly 4.6 ms more per step than at context128, which points at non-projection device work over the long cache (hypothesis; not profiled).

Limits: one model, one GPU, batch 1, primary passage only, no held-out timing, no sanitizer or trace under graph replay, no tail claim. Upstream Marlin and PyTorch graph replay are not ours; the contribution is the corrected kernel's integration, the diagnosis that located the host limit, and the controlled measurement. No default is changed in the repository's kernel code.

## E022 — Decode-step projection time breakdown (diagnostic)

Predeclared 2026-11-10 from `a928561`, requested by the user ("test first") before the report. Diagnostic only: no optimization, kernel, dispatch or model-path change, no end-to-end claim.

Motivation (calculation from E020, not a finding): one decode step takes about 9.9 ms with R and 9.6 ms with Q at context128. R reads 3.09 GB of selected FP16 weights per step, Q about a quarter of that, yet Q is only 3–5% faster. So Q's step is probably not limited by weight traffic. Question: is the Q step limited by host-side per-call launch cost (196 Python/ctypes calls) or by device execution?

Method, `scripts/e022/breakdown.py`, c7, one process: all 196 actual modules with fixed random M=1 FP16 inputs, called once back-to-back per repetition as in a decode step, then one synchronize. 20 warmups + 200 repetitions per path, two reversed path orders. Paths: R module, Q module, raw Q operator call with preallocated output (no wrapper), raw Q call with a synchronize after each call, empty Python loop. Record median host-return time and synchronized total.

Predeclared reading: if Q's host-return time is ≥70% of its synchronized total in both orders, the step is launch/host-bound and reducing per-call launch cost (for example graph replay of the decode step) is the one supported next hypothesis. If ≤40%, it is device-bound and that hypothesis is not supported. Between: inconclusive. No attention/norm/cache work is included, so the sum is not a decode-step time. Smoke: 5 repetitions, inspected before the dependent full job.

### E022 result — Marlin projections are host-bound (2026-11-10, c7, UUID GPU-29e9569a-8a7a-cd23-5443-bd0b72ca241a)

Attempts: smoke 334100 FAILED exit 1 before any timing, from my self-check (it compared raw output with module output minus bias, which is not bitwise in FP16); replaced with the bitwise `raw + bias == module` check and committed. Smoke 334101 COMPLETED and inspected. Full 334102 COMPLETED exit 0; it sat pending for several minutes because I had not released the smoke's retrieval hold, no effect on results. 43 files hash-verified, `results/e022_20261110/`. Identity checks pass in each job; workspace guards intact; raw calls bitwise equal the module computation for all 196 projections.

| 196 M=1 projections, medians of 200, order 1 / order 2 | Host return ms | Total after sync ms | Host share |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| R module (FP16 linear) | 1.932 / 1.933 | 6.959 / 6.960 | 28% |
| Q module (Marlin) | 2.926 / 2.892 | 2.965 / 2.931 | 99% |
| Q raw operator call | 1.708 / 1.697 | 2.497 / 2.497 | 68% |
| Q raw call, synchronize after each | 4.747 / 4.751 | 4.750 / 4.754 | — |
| Empty Python loop | 0.005 | 0.008 | — |

Min–max spread of totals is ≤0.05 ms in every row. Selected weight bytes: R 2,620,391,424, Q 675,569,664.

Predeclared reading: Q module host share is 99% in both orders (≥70%), so the Marlin projection sequence is launch/host-bound; R is device-bound. The supported next hypothesis is reducing per-call host cost, for example graph replay of the Marlin decode step. Not established: any end-to-end gain, capturability of the external launches, or the host/device split of non-projection work. Inputs are random, not model activations; timing of these kernels is treated as data-independent, which is an assumption. Assessment: [BOTTLENECK_ASSESSMENT.md](BOTTLENECK_ASSESSMENT.md).

Decision: diagnostic complete; no optimization implemented. Await a user request for the graph-replay experiment.

## E021 — Acceptance of the Marlin path against the FP64-projection control

Predeclared 2026-11-10 from `1b0cb87`, requested by the user ("go ahead") after E020. This is the THIRD model gate for the same path, written after seeing E017, E018, E019 and E020. The four context128/2048 cases are already known to satisfy it from E019 data, so they are confirmation only. The predictive test is six cases never run in any form: contexts 256, 512 and 1024 × primary/held-out passages. E017 and E019 remain failed under their gates. No timing, kernel, model or tolerance change; E020's timing is not rerun.

Reference: the same model with every projection GEMM accumulated in FP64 on the identical W_hat and rounded to FP16 (E018 mode F64), instead of R's FP16-rounded greedy trajectory. Per case, 64 tokens, `scripts/e021/validate.py`:
1. finite Q logits;
2. Q's greedy tokens equal the control's, or first differ at a step where the control's own logits for the two tokens differ by no more than E017's elementwise bound `0.03+0.01|logit|`; logits are then compared through that step only (later steps have different inputs);
3. Q-versus-control relative L2 over the compared steps ≤ 0.003 (E017's threshold);
4. Q bitwise repeatable, P pass-through bitwise equal to R, workspace guards intact;
5. all 196×64 same-input Q operator calls pass the unchanged GEMM rule against FP64;
6. unchanged `infer.checked` structural state invariants on the Q request.
R and F32 statistics against the control, old-gate counts and Q-versus-R are reported, not gating. Accepted only if all ten cases pass; any failure is recorded as a rejection with no rule edit.

If accepted, the permitted statement is: "Q agrees with a higher-precision evaluation of the same quantized model within the stated bounds on ten requests; under that third, post hoc gate the E020 timing applies to contexts 128 and 2048." Not covered: sanitizer coverage at the new prefill M values 256/512/1024 (operator outputs are FP64-checked on every call; race/memory checks exist only for M1/128/2048), model quality, other models, batch sizes or hardware.

ARC: c7, sbatch+prun, one rank, `scripts/e021/submit.sh`. Smoke is plumbing only: context128 (already seen), 8 tokens; its verdict is not binding. Full job depends on the inspected smoke.

## E020 — Descriptive primary timing of the numerically unaccepted Marlin path

Predeclared 2026-11-10 01:20 EST from `5dcd85c`, before any timing sample exists. After E019's rejection the user was offered stop / descriptive timing / a third gate and delegated the choice ("please figure out the best approach"). Chosen: measure, without changing or adding any acceptance gate. Reason: whether the bridge has any latency benefit is unknown after E017–E019, the node is held, and a measurement labelled as unaccepted misleads nobody, whereas a third post hoc gate would.

Status of the measured path: Q FAILED E017's gate and E019's gate. E020 does not validate it. Any report must present E020 numbers as "latency of a path that is not numerically accepted against the FP16 reference", never as an accepted speedup, and must cite E017/E018/E019 alongside.

Question: on primary requests, is Q's 63-step decode median ≥5% lower than fresh R in both reversed series for a context? Layout is E017's unchanged primary plan: unchanged `infer.py`, separate processes, 3 warmups + 20 requests per cell, series 1 128 R,Q then 2048 R,Q, series 2 reversed, 160 requests, every timed request token/state-checked against R by the unchanged code (primary tokens matched R at both contexts in E019). TTFT, end-to-end and allocator peaks reported ungated. No held-out timing whatever the outcome: the context128 held-out request does not match R's tokens. Any failed per-request check stops the run and is recorded.

Mechanics: `scripts/e019/submit.sh e020 333424` via `inalloc.sh` inside allocation 333424 on c7; own results root `e020-results-333424` with symlinks to E019's passed O/R validation records; `infer.py`'s required `FULL_VALIDATION_INSPECTED` file is created only there and its content states it is not a validation claim. E019's root gets no sentinel.

### E020 result — measured, NOT an accepted speedup (2026-11-10, c7, UUID GPU-29e9569a-8a7a-cd23-5443-bd0b72ca241a, driver 610.57.04, P0)

Attempts: the in-allocation launch at about 08:45 EST failed before any compute because held allocation 333424 had ended at its 4:00 limit at 04:56 while the user's answer was pending (Slurm state TIMEOUT; its recorded smoke `driver.exit` stays 1). c7 was idle, so the identical emitted script was submitted as ordinary batch job 334094 (`sbatch --nodelist=c7`), COMPLETED exit 0. Allocation, one-rank prun, binary/source/pip identity checks pass. All 160 timed requests pass the unchanged per-request token/state checks against R. 103 files hash-verified, `results/e020_20261110/`.

| Context | Series | R decode ms | Q decode ms | Q lower | TTFT Q lower | E2E Q lower | Peak allocated R → Q MiB |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 128 | 1 | 625.716 | 603.211 | 3.597% | 6.176% | 3.660% | 3009 → 1196 |
| 128 | 2 | 626.325 | 597.637 | 4.580% | 5.843% | 4.609% | 3009 → 1196 |
| 2048 | 1 | 908.618 | 645.647 | 28.942% | −3.104% | 23.411% | 3236 → 1383 |
| 2048 | 2 | 909.785 | 645.723 | 29.025% | −2.744% | 23.528% | 3236 → 1383 |

Medians of 20; decode min–max spreads are ≤0.3 ms for R and ≤7.2 ms for Q. Context2048 clears the 5% decode criterion in both series; context128 does not (3.6%/4.6%). Prefill is about 3% slower with Q at 2048. Peak reserved memory is 3248 → 2402 MiB at 128 and 3364 → 2514 MiB at 2048.

Cross-check (observation): R's denominators agree with the independent original-FP16 eager medians from E015 on c9 (622.9/624.1 ms and 907.6/909.7 ms), so the gain is not from a degraded reference.

Unresolved: FP16 decode is about 283 ms slower at context2048 than at 128 although every projection GEMM is M=1 in both; Q is only about 45 ms slower. E020 does not explain this. Weight-memory traffic after the long prefill is a hypothesis, not a finding; no profile was taken.

Limits: Q failed the E017 and E019 numerical gates, so this is the latency of an unaccepted path on two primary requests whose 64 greedy tokens equal R's. One model, one GPU, batch 1, no held-out timing (by design), no tail claim, no whole-model sanitizer or trace. Upstream Marlin supplies the kernel; ours is the synchronization fix, integration, diagnosis and measurement.

Decision: stop. Turning this into an accepted claim needs a user-requested acceptance experiment that does not treat R's greedy tokens as truth, declared before use and disclosed as following E017/E019/E020.

## E018 — Locate the E017 Q/R logit divergence (diagnostic)

Predeclared 2026-11-09 from clean `35cae94`, requested by the user after E017 ("identify ... and bridge the gap", ARC use permitted). Diagnostic only. E017 stays failed and immutable. No timing, no tolerance change, no kernel/dispatch/model change, no passing-subset release.

Offline observation motivating it (existing E017 tensors, CPU only, nothing rerun): Q−R per-position relative L2 is 0.0011–0.0056 with no trend over the 64 positions, prefill position 0.0015. All 591 violations have |R|<1. Positions 19/56 carry a near-uniform shift (mean +0.0184/−0.0134, std 0.0068/0.0075; all 443/107 violations one-signed). Subtracting each position's mean difference leaves 4 violations. E014 C128 (same FP16 weights, no Marlin, compiler reordering) recorded max 0.035–0.039 and relative L2 ≈0.00155, and C2048 failed the same gate with 415 values and exact tokens. These are observations, not a cause.

Hypothesis: the Q/R logit difference is propagated FP16 rounding from individually valid operator results, not an integration defect.

Design, `scripts/e018/diagnose.py`, one process, context128, primary passage, 64 tokens, reusing unchanged E017 `integration.py`/`infer.py`, artifacts, runtime and corrected library from the preserved E017 BeeGFS root (read-only); new output root. Modes switch per request: R; Q (twice); F32 and F64 (same W_hat, projection GEMM accumulated in FP32/FP64 then rounded to FP16, Marlin-free controls); P (R trajectory, every one of the 196×64 projection calls also runs Q on the identical input, both checked against FP64 under the unchanged GEMM rule `abs(err)<=1e-3*abs(ref)+1e-3*rms(ref)`). P's reference is GPU FP64, cross-checked against CPU FP64 on all 196 prefill calls; this deviation from the CPU-reference convention is for runtime only. Block outputs are recorded per forward for per-block divergence. Fresh R/Q are compared with the saved E017 tensors.

Predeclared reading:
- Supported: zero Q failures over all 12,544 same-input calls, AND a Marlin-free control (F32 or F64 versus R) reaches at least half of Q-versus-R relative L2 under the unchanged logit gate.
- Refuted: any same-input Q operator failure, Q nondeterminism, or workspace-guard damage; report the first failing layer/step and stop.
- Otherwise inconclusive.
Whether the unchanged 0.03+0.01|R| gate also rejects the Marlin-free controls is reported, not a criterion. No outcome accepts E017 or releases timing; any later gate/timing experiment needs its own predeclaration.

ARC: sbatch+prun, one rank, c7 preferred (node-local E017 venv). An 8-token smoke job with identity checks (allocation, launcher, binary/source/pip hashes) is inspected before the dependent 64-token job. Home is at quota, so the script is embedded in the batch file and results stay on BeeGFS until retrieved.

### E018 result — hypothesis supported (2026-11-09, c7, UUID GPU-29e9569a-8a7a-cd23-5443-bd0b72ca241a, driver 610.57.04)

Attempts: smoke 333407 FAILED exit1 after R/Q/Q2 from a diagnostic-script bug (`'F32'.rstrip('2')` selected a nonexistent mode); no result used. Fixed and committed before resubmission; smoke 333409 COMPLETED (8 tokens) and inspected, dependent full 333413 COMPLETED in 2m42s. All three preserved under `results/e018_20261109/`; 44 retrieved files match the BeeGFS hashes (`retrieved.sha256`). Allocation, single-rank prun, E017 binary (3), source (80) and pip-freeze identities pass in each job.

Observations, context128 primary, 64 tokens:

| Versus R, unchanged E017 gate | Violations | Max abs | Relative L2 | Tokens |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Q (corrected Marlin), run twice, bitwise repeatable | 591 | 0.0547 | 0.0022367 | equal |
| F32 control, no Marlin | 667 | 0.0515 | 0.0022444 | equal |
| F64 control, no Marlin | 102 | 0.0449 | 0.0022569 | equal |
| R repeat / P pass-through | 0 | 0 | 0 | bitwise |

- Fresh R and fresh Q logits are bitwise equal to the preserved E017 tensors, so this is the same failure, reproduced.
- Same-input operator check: 12,544 calls (196 modules × prefill M128 + 63 decode M1), 123,217,920 outputs, zero Q and zero R violations of the unchanged GEMM rule. Relative L2 to FP64: Q median 2.0694e-4, max 3.7258e-4; R median 2.0694e-4, max 3.7258e-4. GPU and CPU FP64 references agree exactly on all 196 prefill calls. Workspace guards intact.

Interpretation under the predeclared reading: supported. No integration or operator defect is found at any of the 196 modules with real activations; the Marlin-free controls reach 100.3%/100.9% of Q-versus-R relative L2 and themselves fail the E017 gate. That gate therefore cannot distinguish corrected Marlin from a higher-precision evaluation of the same weights; in this request R is the farthest of the three from the F64 control.

Limits: one request, context128, primary passage, one node. Context2048, held-out passage, complete-model trace and whole-model sanitizers remain unrun. F64 changes only the 196 projection GEMMs; it is not a full-FP64 model oracle. E017 remains failed under its declared gate and no timing exists.

Decision: stop. Revisit E017 acceptance only through a new predeclared experiment whose model gate is calibrated against Marlin-free controls (candidate: Q must pass the unchanged per-operator rule on every call and be no farther from F64 than R is, plus exact tokens), disclosed as chosen after the E017 failure.

## E017 — Corrected Marlin in the pinned full model

Predeclared 2026-11-09 from clean `b867a13`, explicitly requested by the user. E017 is the next unused number. This integration comparison supersedes the prior stop only for this bounded task. Every historical implementation, measurement and manifest remains immutable. Upstream Marlin supplies compressed Tensor Core execution; our contributions are the synchronization correction, integration, validation and controlled comparison. No speedup will be attributed to adding the barrier alone.

Hypothesis: replacing supported transformer-block linear weights with the identical G128 quantized representation through unchanged corrected E006 A lowers 63-step decode median by at least 5% in both reversed primary series for a context, and reduces steady resident tensor storage versus reconstructed FP16. Latency and memory decisions are independent. No kernel edit, tuning, tile/dispatch change, compiler, speculation, new graph capture, attention change or alternate backend.

Coverage chosen from pinned config and actual source constraints: all seven `torch.nn.Linear` projections in each of28 transformer blocks (196): q/o K1536,N1536; k/v K1536,N256; gate/up K1536,N8960; down K8960,N1536. Inspect and assert actual module names/shapes/counts before replacement. E005 C ABI accepts positive M, K>=256 divisible128, N divisible64; reuse its original A dispatcher for prefill M128/M2048 and decode M1. Model embeddings and tied LM head remain original FP16, as do all biases, RMS norms, RoPE, attention, FP16 static KV caches, masks, tokenizer and fixed greedy/EOS policy. No hidden fallback: unsupported selected shapes fail, never call the wheel GEMM. Bias is an explicit FP16 addition after the FP16 GEMM in O/R/Q, including the original-weight reference, to separate quantization from a bias-fusion difference. Report this common operation boundary.

Quantize the loaded original FP16 block weights ONCE deterministically on CPU under the project contract: stored FP16(maxabs/7), FP32 division and nearest-even integer rounding/clamp[-7,7], q=z+8, G128 along K, then FP16((q-8)*scale). Preserve project packing/scales, source and operand hashes, lossless Marlin weight/scale roundtrips and finite reconstructed weights. Do not substitute another checkpoint/quantizer. Save artifacts in isolated BeeGFS storage. Reference R uses reconstructed FP16 W_hat through ordinary PyTorch FP16 linear GEMM with reduced-precision reduction/TF32 disabled. Candidate Q uses the identical codes/scales through preserved E006 library SHA e4eca5e30436c547358a9c8125d69526c6bb3318da034afd54ada6744181e2b7, modeA, original dispatcher and FP32 global reduction. Official wheel is used for lossless packing only. Original FP16 O is separately evaluated to describe quantization changes, never as sole Q correctness oracle or a two-passage quality benchmark.

First prerequisite: actual original-model `model.layers.0.mlp.down_proj` inputs captured during prefill and decode. Verify original layer inventory, runtime/binary/source identities, common layout and bias semantics, roundtrip codes/scales/reconstructed bits, all-output FP64 GEMM threshold abs(error)<=1e-3*abs(ref)+1e-3*rms(ref), repeated poisoned output calls, guard/lock reset, correct workspace capacity and actual corrected-Marlin symbols/resources. Check M1/M128/M2048 numerical/memcheck/synccheck, with racecheck representative M1/M128 (M2048 shares the large-M body; do not claim complete M2048 race coverage). Preserve inputs/outputs/FP64 references/statistics before assertions. A failure or incomplete prerequisite stops integration/performance, retaining diagnostics; no alternative mechanism. Extend operator numerical/sanitizer checks to distinct declared shape/dispatch families before full-model acceptance. Source inspection identifies N256 low-parallelism fallback and two1024-row Marlin launches for M2048,N8960. Dry queries describe only the first split; actual trace counts must enumerate both. Original A remains unchanged.

Full model paths are separate processes with identical original non-replaced parameters and artifact hashes. Q drops all selected original/reconstructed FP16 GPU weights after replacement. R retains only selected W_hat, not compressed GPU copies. Inventory actual unique tensor storage, including tied aliases, persistent guarded workspace and cache, and record allocator allocated/reserved/peaks separately; empty unused allocator cache before warmed measurements and preserve cold/setup costs. Repacking/quantization/model load and replacement/setup occur outside warmed requests and have separate timers. A common eager static-cache request loop retains all position logits in GPU buffers in BOTH paths and transfers them only outside timers; no new capture/compilation.

Full validation requires BOTH contexts128/2048 and primary/held-out/primary/held-out requests after resets for O/R/Q, 64 fixed outputs with prefill supplying token1,63 cached forwards. Save all candidate/reference tensors, tokens, failed indices and diagnostics before numerical assertions. Q versus R requires finite logits, exact64 greedy token IDs, abs(delta)<=0.03+0.01*abs(R) for EVERY logit and relativeL2<=0.003. Repeated same-path requests must be deterministic; check every recorded device position/feedback token, every layer's cache occupied length/reset state/stable addresses and unchanged parameter/buffer hashes. Cache values across Q/R may differ within propagated numerical error; do not require cache bit identity as a substitute for logit gates. O versus R reports differences separately without equivalence/quality acceptance claims. Any numerical/token/launcher/sanitizer failure stops all performance release, including passing subsets.

Only after full validation/inspection, collect one short separate actual model trace per context/path (8 fixed outputs) proving all196 selected projections invoke Q in prefill/decode, correct symbols/resources/attention and no wheel GEMM/compile/graph fallback. Module flags alone do not prove execution. Keep trace instrumentation outside timings; exact same arithmetic/storage/modules run validation/timing. Retained project build/CTest/check_inblock checks remain separate from operator sanitizer coverage and whole-model validation.

Fresh matched timing:3 full request warmups and20 requests per variant/context/series, fresh process per cell; series1 (128,R),(128,Q),(2048,R),(2048,Q), series2 exact reverse. Preserve all160 primary samples and checks; no trimming. Synchronized wall decode starts after token1 staging and includes63 cached forwards, masks/position/feedback/logit copies and final synchronization. TTFT starts before prompt tokenization/H2D/cache reset and ends at CPU-visible token1; complete request includes final D2H and detokenization. CPU diagnostic tensor transfers/hashes happen after timers in both paths. Report cold request, load/quantization/repacking/replacement/setup, TTFT/E2E, warmed allocator memory and explicit resident storage. Only fresh same-series R medians are denominators; no historical GEMM/inference timing. Positive gain=100*(1-Q/R). Predeclared 2026-11-09 from clean `514e45b`, explicitly authorized by the user's request to implement direct graph replay. This supersedes the E014 stop instruction only for this new mechanism. Setup330278 completes in3m21s on c4 and pinned model download/hash succeeds. The new `/tmp/e014-hpuvvad-20261108` environment has torch2.14.0+cu130/Transformers4.57.1 and an exact resolved freeze. The common explicit loop uses one enforced SDPA memory-efficient backend for all paths, with TF32 and reduced-precision FP16 reductions disabled. Explicit padded additive causal masks support the same backend for dynamic and static caches, avoid efficient-attention bias-stride restrictions, and disable the framework's GQA fast path consistently. This is a supported fused attention implementation, not a claim to the fastest available baseline. A different attention implementation would be a separate study. Actual traces contain memory-efficient attention kernels, GEMV, elementwise/norm/rotary work, cache copies and no graph launches or compiled regions. Seven decoded tokens launch8799 kernels in each trace. Short-context decode's106.20ms CPU-marked window has63.74% GPU interval union,8826 internal gaps and3391 adjacent kernel pairs whose next CPU launch starts after the prior kernel finishes. Long-context decode's104.45ms window is95.68% GPU-busy, with140 such late pairs. These profiler-perturbed intervals support a short-context execution-overhead opportunity, without estimating attainable speedup or establishing a unique bottleneck. Prefill is18.09/180.71ms in the trace, distinct from unprofiled timings. One boundary cudaDeviceSynchronize and21 cudaMemcpyAsync calls occur in each decode trace. Raw records preserve all allocation/copy APIs. The initial offline parser sees both CPU and mirrored GPU annotation names, rejects a duplicate marker, and is corrected to select CPU user_annotation explicitly. Add a test for mirrored annotations and preserve the raw trace. No GPU replay is needed for that reporting correction.

Comparison now authorized by the inspected diagnostic: A dynamic KV/eager decode, B static KV/eager decode, C the same static path with `torch.compile(mode="reduce-overhead",fullgraph=True,dynamic=False)` around forward plus greedy argmax only. Prefill, explicit mask construction, token-buffer copies and host loop remain common/eager. B has no automatic compilation because the harness does not use generate. A/B/C share model/tokenizer/FP16 weights, SDPA backend, prompts, masking semantics, position IDs and greedy fixed-length decoding. Static capacity is context+64, reused/reset between requests, with allocation/setup cost recorded outside warmed request timing. A creates a fresh DynamicCache per request. Static reset costs remain inside the request. Static paths may process masked unused cache positions. C bundles upstream operator fusion, code generation, scheduling/kernel selection and any actual CUDA graph replay, so B/C is not a pure launch-gap ablation. Require fullgraph compilation with no suppressed errors, record compiler counters, graph breaks/recompilations, capture one short warmed C timeline per context and verify actual graph replay or its documented absence before timing. No alternative compile-mode search or backend fallback.

TTFT starts before repeated prompt tokenization, input H2D and cache creation/reset, ending when the first token is available on CPU. Request E2E ends after all64 tokens are copied to CPU and detokenized. Record preparation, prefill-to-first, post-first boundary and output time separately so component boundaries sum to E2E. There is one first-token synchronization, not an artificial per-token host synchronization. Fixed64 generation continues through EOS, recording every EOS position. This workload does not measure natural-EOS service, transport or a multiuser scheduler.

## E012 — Bounded source-PC attribution of corrected Marlin

Status: complete bounded diagnostic, 2026-11-07, starting from clean `460ecc0`. Evidence checkpoint `181f6fe`. Instruction work is quantified. Question and hypothesis: Does the additional M128 instruction work reside primarily in the main-loop MMA/data preparation, or in block/global reduction, epilogue and lock waiting? E011 observes approximately 3.03× executed warp instructions, 16× Tensor instructions and nearly unchanged DRAM reads. The working hypothesis is that main-loop work accounts for most of the additional instructions. An alternative is substantial coordination/epilogue work. Sampled consumer-PC stalls can localize waiting but cannot uniquely identify the producer or establish critical-path time.

Fixed scope: corrected A only, M4/M128 and K/N=4096/11008 and 11008/4096. Prefer separate unpatched warp-sampling profiles for waits and instruction-level executed-count profiles for dynamic work. Static opcode counts alone cannot identify dynamic hot paths. The instruction table separates known source regions from uncertain caller ownership. Counts are exact exported warp counts, while region assignment is a conservative compiler-source model.

Instruction counts are warp-level counts ignoring instruction predicates. Millions mean 10^6. Shares describe diagnostic instruction counts, not runtime. Known regions exclude ambiguous inline callers.

| K / N | M | Warp instructions M | Known main loop % | Known coordination/epilogue % | Ambiguous phase % | Source coverage % |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 4096 / 11008 | 4 | 3.606077 | 49.82 | 1.82 | 43.80 | 100.00 |

1. **More executed work is observed, with bounded attribution.** Total instruction counts grow 3.606077→10.958725 million and 3.538074→10.702177 million. Known main-loop counts rise by 5139968/5086344 instructions, accounting for 69.91%/71.00% of the net increases. However, 43.80–44.77% of M4 counts and 31.43–31.80% of M128 counts have ambiguous phase ownership. Assigning all ambiguous M4 counts to the main loop and none of the ambiguous M128 counts gives lower bounds of only 48.42%/48.89% of the total increase. Those bounds cross the 50% majority criterion, so the majority explanation is not established. The result favors it among classified sites rather than rejecting the alternative. Source-correlated HMMA counts are exactly 176128 at M4 and 2818048 at M128 in both orientations, matching E011's independently collected Tensor instruction counts. Count agreement does not establish unperturbed timing.

2. **Source coverage is not phase coverage.** Every counted instruction has a source location, but CUDA half-precision intrinsic sites often lack an unambiguous caller. Many unassigned sites contain HMUL2/HADD2/HFMA2 instructions. CLI exports retain all inline locations, whereas the report API supplies a primary location. We join both by the exact reported PC, count each PC once, and retain conflicting phases rather than choosing a favorable frame or nearest source line. The archived header hash and region boundaries are checked. Known coordination/epilogue instructions comprise 1.15–2.21% of these diagnostic counts. This is neither their runtime fraction nor a bound on critical-path coordination cost. Counts ignore instruction predicates. Compiler-correlated FP16-branch sites therefore do not demonstrate that the passing FP32 reduction contract changed.

3. **The sampling branch is inconclusive.** Every capture exceeds 1000 samples and reports one pass/32-cycle interval/32 MiB buffer/zero overflow. Nevertheless, all report dropped bytes, from 72292 to 598942. Zero overflow does not establish lossless capture. M128 also misses the 95% source-coverage gate. All unmapped sampled mass is at `0xffffffffffffffff`, with no source/SASS information. Its meaning is unresolved and it remains in the denominator. Do not identify it with a particular stall reason, discard it to pass the gate, convert dropped bytes into a missing-sample fraction, or use stable-looking reversed-series shares as evidence that capture loss is unbiased. Saved stall shares are diagnostic records with failed-quality flags, not an accepted bottleneck attribution.

The sampling table records why the predeclared gate fails. It deliberately reports no accepted stall-time decomposition.

Sampling uses one pass at 32 cycles with a 32 MiB buffer. All eight captures fail the zero-dropped-data gate. M128 also fails 95% source coverage. These counts document a measurement limitation, not accepted stall attribution.

|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|

| 4096 / 11008 | 128 | 1 | 772262 | 587751 | 66931 | 91.33 |

| 4096 / 11008 | 4 | 2 | 181284 | 79265 | 8996 | 95.04 |

4. **What worked and what did not.** Exact-binary graph-node application replay supports both instruction-level counts and unpatched PC sampling here. Full-output checks pass on all twelve executions. Source/API imports, complete PC instances, inline-context deduplication and the corruption-data checker work. The initial API package-path assumption fails because CUDA's `ncu` is a wrapper. Its corrected installation path is verified. The first queued-node decision and later stale queue update are superseded by scheduler accounting. An early retrieval is extracted before transfer completion, then corrected with exit-status/gzip checks. The final retrieval preserves the incomplete early `identity-measurement.log` snapshot separately. All completed first-profile/validation/raw records remain unchanged. Fixed 32-cycle sampling fails loss/source-coverage criteria. These are measurement limitations, not demonstrated incorrect outputs or a kernel race.

5. **Decision and revisit conditions.** E012 closes without an implementation change. Keep corrected E006 A and preserve the earlier negative results. The unresolved question remains how much M128 main-loop versus reduction/epilogue/lock work lies on the critical path. Source instruction shares and these lossy captures cannot answer it. A separately authorized quality-focused retry would first need a small pilot with no dropped data and an explicit audit of the all-ones PC, followed by a predeclared fixed series if its gate passes. Better caller/CFG evidence is needed before tightening instruction ownership. Neither a partition rewrite nor a dispatch policy follows from the present record. Do not repeat this unchanged interval or silently relax the quality thresholds.

Verification and preservation: complete and corruption-data analyzers pass locally and on ARC. They reject duplicate PCs, changed counts/source phases/hashes/templates, wrong targets, failed launches and missing reports. They explicitly retain all eight sampling quality failures and the all-ones PC samples. The analysis checks exact API/CSV units, aggregate/PC-count agreement, every selected output and frozen measured tooling. Raw profile hashes pass before/after CPU analysis. CPU analysis 329259 passes in five seconds. Derived phase-key ordering is then made deterministic across Python hash seeds, with that initial successful analysis preserved. Final CPU analysis 329277 also passes in five seconds, with unchanged raw profiles. This formatting correction changes no counter value or GPU capture. c54 executes only CPU report import/query/analysis tasks, never the sm120 kernel. It requests one CPU/task, while Slurm allocates the whole 16-CPU node. No user-owned allocation is cancelled. All job IDs and scheduler states are historical.

Limits: one GPU, two rectangles, two M values, four synthetic matrices and one instruction-count capture per case. Matching cyclic accesses do not guarantee identical cache lines or clocks. Profiler instrumentation and sampling buffers add overhead. Patched counts can perturb execution, especially lock spinning. Warp sampling has no phase time resolution and locates consumers rather than uniquely identifying producers. The five requested not-issued indicators do not cover every scheduler state. ## E011 — Endpoint profiles with preserved cyclic weight access

Status: complete, 2026-11-07, starting from clean `dd4505a`; complete measurement/analysis checkpoint `ca574cc`. Eight complete profiles and both timing series pass. Decision: no kernel change justified yet. Only this bounded profiling experiment is authorized. Corrected E006 A and E004 pre-materialized FP16 cuBLAS remain unchanged. No kernel change, C configuration, tuning, dispatch, unfused comparator, additional batch or shape is included.

Question and competing hypotheses: E010's four-matrix advantage narrows from 3.6–4.4x at M4 to 1.02–1.20x at M128. Additional instruction/compute work per similar serviced weight traffic could reduce Marlin's advantage. Alternatively repeated traffic, memory waits, shape-dependent cuBLAS dispatch, available work, or Marlin partition/reduction work could explain some of the change. These are hypotheses. Neither occupancy limits nor equal latency identifies a bottleneck.

Fixed design and success criterion: Collect exactly eight implementation/shape/M cases, corrected A and pre-materialized W_hat cuBLAS, M4/M128, K/N 4096/11008 and 11008/4096. Reuse E010 capacity 128, four independent weights/seeds 3–6, common nested activation prefixes, original sequential order, mode 16 and numerical contract. A successful explanatory experiment preserves access context on every collection pass, verifies complete operation coverage and all selected outputs, provides supported targeted metrics with units, and distinguishes observations from interpretations and remaining uncertainty. No optimization acceptance threshold applies because no implementation changes. Keep E010 historical. Fresh context is exactly two independent reversed endpoint series, ten complete-cycle warmups and 100 samples per row, 16 timing rows total with fresh matched denominators. No outlier trimming.

Selected method after installed-capability inspection: Application replay with cache control none, clock control none, dynamic Tensor-pipeline boost state, strict grid/order matching, graph-node profiling and the fourth GEMM of the final cycle of the E010 100-cycle graph. Every pass reruns complete setup, ten direct complete-cycle warmups, and all 99 preceding graph cycles plus the first three operations of the last cycle. This refines the provisional eleventh-cycle target before any profile is collected, to preserve E010's complete graph/event sequence. Trace both implementations first to determine every kernel in a GEMM. Select by deterministic operation position, never latency. A CUDA profiler start/stop window around a complete cycle plus launch skip/count can select the final operation's complete kernel sequence without altering its graph dependencies. This retains one-stream graph execution and workspace behavior but profiler instrumentation, node serialization and collection overhead can differ from E010 graph-event timing. Verify actual installed support before collection. If unsupported, document the closest defensible method before proceeding. Do not collect overlapping alternative replay modes without a specific unresolved question.

Alternatives considered: Isolated kernel replay does not re-execute intervening weights and is unsuitable as a direct E010 memory-state measurement. Application-range replay preserves complete execution and handles multi-kernel operations but aggregates resources and may restrict source metrics. ### E011 final measurements and decision

**The endpoint narrowing repeats. The profiles support increased instruction/Tensor work over nearly constant Marlin DRAM reads, together with shape-dependent cuBLAS selection and service. They do not isolate a change worth implementing. Keep corrected A unchanged.**

Completed evidence: `ca574cc`, following criteria `2a2e44d`, measured harness `76b6742`, workflow `aac531f`, import fix `ac318bb` and bounded resume `4c0409c`. Raw records are in [results/e011_c4_20261107](../results/e011_c4_20261107/); reproduce with [scripts/e011](../scripts/e011/README.md). No earlier source or result is replaced.

Resume 329113 completes the remaining three profiles on c4 in 15m28s, exit 0. It rechecks the same GPU UUID/driver, source/binaries, exact dependencies, six mapped libraries and frozen tooling before and after. Both complete and negative-data analyzers pass on ARC and locally. All eight reports contain 13 validated application passes per kernel: 104 complete application executions across eight cases, with both kernels covered in the one split-K operation. The interrupted sixth-case attempt remains separate and contributes no counters. All previously saved raw records remain byte-identical. The sole derived sample-statistics difference between ARC Python 3.11 and local Python 3.10 is at most 8.89e-16 us in standard deviation; both exports are retained.

Validation: every target passes full-output FP64, exact E010 operand/output/resource identity, three direct/three poisoned graph cycles, inactive-row and workspace/lock guards. Maximum absolute error is 0.001968023826520948 and relative L2 is 0.000208206180746902, within the unchanged elementwise contract. Reuse E010's same-environment numerical/sanitizer scope; no fresh sanitizer claim is made. All eight complete-cycle traces use one device/kernel stream. No intended inter-kernel concurrency is removed. Local host build/CTest and target-plan/parser corruption checks pass; CPU checks alone are not CUDA validation.

Fresh application context contains 16 timing rows, 1600 complete cycles and 6400 timed GEMMs, ten warmups/100 samples per row, two independent reversed series, no artificial flush. For K/N=4096/11008, speedups are 4.392/4.424× at M4 and 1.027/1.032× at M128. For K/N=11008/4096 they are 3.629/3.627× and 1.183/1.209×. M128 gains are 2.667/3.114% versus 15.494/17.311%; the first rectangle remains below the earlier 5% practical-gain gate. Largest matched median drift is 1.309%. All raw samples, p90, ranges and sample standard deviations are retained without trimming. These two series do not establish confidence intervals or stable tail latency.

Application values are cycle median/4 in us, series 1 / series 2. Profiler duration is the sum of selected sequential kernel durations, excluding gaps. Traffic and instructions cover the fixed seed 6 operation, not all four matrices. MiB means 2^20 bytes and instruction millions mean 10^6. Tensor activity and eligible warps remain per kernel, in GEMM/reduction order for the two-kernel operation. Percentages are not peak FLOP utilization.

| K / N | M | Implementation | Application us/GEMM S1 / S2 | Profiler sum us | DRAM read / write MiB | L2 MiB | Warp inst M | Tensor active % | Eligible warps |
